## Projectiles and impact effects (settled 2026-09-15)

What a round does on arrival, settled for the mesh viewer's Thompson and
Bazooka: the effect frame, the bullet-hole decal, the damage falloff, and the
emitter/particle vocabulary. Full write-up with every address in
[subsystems/projectiles-and-impacts.md](subsystems/projectiles-and-impacts.md);
viewer code in `viewer/effects-core.js`, `viewer/effects.js`, `bf42/effects.py`.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| IMP-1 | An impact effect is stood up with **Up = the surface normal**; Right = Up x DOF, DOF = Right x Up, Right = Up x DOF, with DOF the fresh object's world +Z. The bullet hole therefore lies flat in the wall | **verified** | `Game::playCollisionEffect` client 0x0040e590 / lnxded 0x0805de20 writes the normal into transform row 1 then calls `makeOrthonormalBasis` client 0x0040e360 / lnxded 0x08061bd0 (both decompiled, identical) |
| IMP-2 | The effect for a hit is `MaterialManager.setEffectTemplate(attacker = ProjectileTemplate.material, defender = struck material)`; the projectile's `material` replaces the collision material before the lookup | **verified** | `Projectile::handleCollision` lnxded 0x0831ee80 (+0x88 override), `GameServer::handleCollisionForProjectile` 0x08153ba0 -> `MaterialManager::getEffectTemplate(att, def, \|v.n\|)` 0x081750d0. Per-cell `map<float,…>` keyed by the clamped cosine (`MMCell::getEffectTemplate` 0x081746a0) — one entry per cell in vanilla, so inert |
| IMP-3 | The bullet hole is a mesh *particle* — `Fx_Richo*Decal`, geometry `Decal_*_m1` (0.2 m quad in the XZ plane), lifted `relativePositionInUp 0.001`, `timeToLive` uniform 1..15 s, `alphaOverTime 0/1\|70/1\|100/0` — not a `DecalManager` decal | **verified** | data (`Objects/Effects/e_Decal_*/effects.con`, `Common/effects.con` composites `RichoStoneDecal` etc.); mesh particle scale/alpha path `Particle::handleUpdate` lnxded 0x0820ad20; `DecalManager` 0x081e05f0 has no impact caller |
| IMP-4 | Mesh-particle alpha goes to `IStandardMesh::setAlpha` as a byte, so with the decal's `.rs` `alphaTestRef 0.5` the hole vanishes at 50% opacity rather than fading to nothing | **verified** (server) | `Particle::handleUpdate` 0x0820ad20: `255 * alphaOverTime(phase)` -> QI IID_IStandardMesh -> vtable+0x34. Client `Particle::handleUpdate` not isolated |
| IMP-5 | `sizeModifier` is the mesh-particle scale switch: (0,0,0) = draw authored size; else scale = `size x sizeOverTime x sizeModifier` | **verified** | same function; default (0,0,0) in `ParticleTemplate` ctor lnxded 0x0820b000 and client `makeScript` 0x005384d0 (+0x7c..+0x84 written only when non-zero) |
| IMP-6 | Damage falloff: full to `distToStartLoseDamage`, linear to `minDamage x full` at `distToMinDamage`, flat after; skipped when `minDamage >= 1` or start `<= 0`. Base = `materialDamage` of the projectile's material | **verified** | `Projectile::getDamage` client 0x00542e80 / lnxded 0x0831f3c0, both decompiled. Client offsets +0x278/+0x27c/+0x280 from the console accessors 0x004da7f0/0x004da9a0/0x004dab50 |
| IMP-7 | `gravityModifier` defaults to 1.0 and scales -14.73 m/s^2; the projectile's own update integrates nothing (physics body does), it only runs the `explodeNearEnemyDistance` fuse | **verified** | `ProjectileTemplate` ctor lnxded 0x0831f8d0 (+0x164 = 1.0), client `makeScript` 0x00541a60 (+0x214 written when != 1.0); `Projectile::handleUpdate` 0x0831e940 |
| CRD-1 | `CRD_UNIFORM/a/b` samples `a + r(b-a)`, r in (0,1] — the two numbers are the ends in the order written (`15/1` = 1..15); `CRD_EXPONENTIAL/a` = `-a ln r`; `CRD_NORMAL/a/b` = `a + b N(0,1)`; the 4th field mirrors the sign with p = 1/2 | **verified** | `Random::getContinuousRandom` lnxded 0x081e28b0; emitter inline copy `Emitter::calcInvItensity` 0x081e2f10 (mirror at 0x081e3037); the client's twins, `0x004f5930` (the mirror) and `0x004f58d0` (the per-distribution switch), are identical (2026-09-16) |
| EMT-1 | Emitter spawns are spaced `\|1/intensity\|` apart, intensity resampled per spawn and scaled by `speed / IntensityAtSpeed` when set; zero intensity = one per 100 s; `looping` restarts on expiry | **verified** | `Emitter::calcInvItensity` 0x081e2f10, `Emitter::handleUpdate` 0x081e3200 (+0x110 age, +0x114 next) |
| EMT-2 | The first spawn is at t = 0 | **verified** (2026-09-16; viewer fixed 2026-09-16) | The constructors (lnxded 0x081e2bb0, 0x081e2c40) zero age (+0x110) and next (+0x114), and `Emitter::handleUpdate` spawns when `age >= next` (0x081e37bb–0x081e37d0, `fxch` then `fucompp`), so the first tick is due. That test is reached only when the template's `showInFirstPerson` (+0x417) and the instance's +0x124 are both zero: the +0x104 gate always jumps to 0x081e58c6, which re-enters at 0x081e356d, and either flag set sends the update down a separate path to its return (0x081e3579–0x081e3680, not read) — the old "no spawn when set" reading, confirmed. Nothing found sets +0x124. The burst ends when `age >= timeToLive` (0x081e3305). On the tick a `delay` runs out, age grows by the delay's pre-tick value, not by the leftover (0x081e32ae–0x081e32ce). `viewer/effects-core.js` now matches the engine on both edges (fixed 2026-09-16): it carries forward the delay's pre-tick value rather than the leftover, and ends a burst strictly before `age == ttl`, not at it |
| EMT-3 | `startRotation` rolls the emitter frame about its DOF per spawn; units degrees | **verified** (2026-09-16) | `dice::ref2::roll` 0x08061df0 = `rotateAboutLine(m, m+0x20 (row 2), angle)` at spawn site 0x081e522e, the angle drawn from the CRD at template +0x400 that `makeScript` names `ObjectTemplate.startRotation` (0x081e6a6c). It passes unchanged through `rotateAboutLine` 0x08061e10, `setRotateAboutLine` 0x080621b0 and `rotateZDeg` 0x080625f0 into `setRotateZDeg` 0x08062740, which multiplies by π/180 (0x086b1ca4) before `fsincos`: degrees. Sprite `initRotation` / `rotationSpeed` (client-only code) remain inferred degrees |
| EMT-4 | Spawn offset/velocity are `relativePositionIn*` / `positionalSpeedIn*` along the (rolled) frame; `addEmitterSpeed` adds the emitter's velocity x `emitterSpeedScale` | **verified** | spawn block 0x081e40a3-0x081e4e9d (`SpriteParticleNew::addParticle` call 0x081e4d8f), template offsets from `EmitterTemplate::makeScript` 0x081e61c0 / client 0x005097a0 |
| EMT-5 | The drag law is `v *= e^(-drag dt)` | **refuted** (2026-09-16) | A particle's body is a `PointPhysicsNode`: `Particle::handleUpdate` lnxded 0x0820ad20 calls its `setDrag` (vtable +0x94) and `setGravityModifier` (+0xb4) every tick. `PointPhysicsNode::updatePhysics` (client 0x00578ca0, lnxded 0x082562c0) adds drag to the acceleration once per tick — `accel −= (scale·v − wind)·π·r²·drag/mass` (0x00578990), `r` = `getBoundingRadius()`, `scale` = `1 + 24·min(underWater/r, 1)` — then integrates four semi-implicit sub-steps of dt/4 (0x00578aa0). Nothing multiplies velocity by an exponential. Same law as vehicles, [physics.md](subsystems/physics.md) §3. `viewer/effects-core.js` `integrateParticle` still uses the exponential, pending the mass and radius a spawned particle's body reports |
| SPR-1 | The server simulates sprite particles | **refuted** (2026-09-16) | lnxded `dice::ref2::geom::ParticleSystem::setTransformation`, `draw`, `isSupported`, `addParticle` and `update` (0x083a9300–0x083a9354) are empty, and `SpriteParticleNew::handleUpdate` 0x0820c650 / `addParticle` 0x0820c730 call them directly. The sprite-particle update exists only in the client |
| SPR-2 | How the client makes a sprite particle system | **verified** (2026-09-16) | `SpriteParticleNewTemplate`'s ctor (0x00568670) owns a `geom::ParticleSystemTemplate` (0x00618350) at +0x60. `createObject` (0x00568720, vtable 0x008fbf90 +0x2c) builds a `SpriteParticleNew` (0x005685c0), which asks that template to `createGeometry` (0x006189a0, vtable 0x0091516c +0x18 — the server's `createGeometry` sits at the same index) and keeps the resulting `geom::ParticleSystem` (0x0060afe0, vtable 0x00914c14) at +0xf0. `SpriteParticleNew::addParticle` (0x00567c00) forwards to it |
| SPR-3 | When a sprite particle's values are chosen | **verified** at spawn; per-frame **inferred** (2026-09-16) | `geom::ParticleSystem::addParticle` (0x0060a680) rolls ten CRDs once, at spawn — `initRotation`, `initAnimationFrame`, `animationSpeed`, `XYSizeRatio`, `rotationSpeed` and five fields no sprite property names — through the client CRD evaluator. The `…OverTime` curves are evaluated per frame in `draw` (0x0060a0e0): it splits each particle's phase into an index and a fraction, passes `colorRGBAOverTime` to 0x00609e10 and reads four floats back, and indexes `rotationSpeedOverTime`'s samples inline. That path was not traced to the finished quad |
| SPR-4 | Sprite `…OverTime` curves are rasterised to 101 samples at load, like mesh particles' | **verified** (2026-09-16) | The `ParticleSystemTemplate` ctor 0x00618350 gives each curve its own 101-sample array: `XYSizeRatioOverTime`, `rotationSpeedOverTime`, `animationSpeedOverTime` and `alphaTestRefOverTime` seeded 1.0, and four `colorRGBAOverTime` channels (from +0xb8c) seeded 255.0 |
| SPR-5 | How the blend mode is applied | **confirmed** (2026-09-17) | BM names are D3DBLEND ordinals 1–11 (identity: `0x005bd830` / `0x005241c0` / `0x00524340`). Ctor defaults src=5 dest=6 (`0x00618350`). Stored at template +0x5c4/+0x5c8 by `setSrcBlendMode`/`setDestBlendMode` (`0x006182b0`/`0x006182e0`). Those fields are +0x14/+0x18 of an embedded block at template +0x5b0. `addParticle` (`0x0060a680`) passes `template+0x5b0` into the particle ctor (`0x00609ea0`), which stores it at particle +0x78 (stack slot proved). Draw queues the particle's +0x34 object (vtable `0x00914c00`); flush calls slot 0 `0x0062e870` → `0x0062cf20`, which does `SetRenderState(D3DRS_SRCBLEND/DESTBLEND, *(particle+0x78)+0x14/+0x18)` via device +0xc8 — i.e. the template's own stored ordinals, no remap. Viewer `CustomBlending` + `D3D_BLEND_FACTOR` matches. `makeScript` (`0x00567c30`) skips *emitting* `destBlendMode` when *src* == 6 (serialize quirk). Data: `BMInvSourceAlpha` 3,554, `BMOne` 3,052, unset 509, `BMDestAlpha` 25 of 7,159 templates |
| SPR-6 | Our sprites need only `size`, colour, rotation and a blend mode | **refuted; now implemented (2026-09-16)** | `SpriteParticleNewTemplate::makeScript` reads `numAnimationFrames`, `initAnimationFrame`, `animationSpeed` and `animationSpeedOverTime`, and `addParticle` rolls the middle two per particle: texture-atlas flipbooks. 791 of 7,159 sprite templates across 18 mods set more than one frame (705 at 16, 80 at 4), among them `fx_expl_core`, `fx_blood02` and the aircraft engine fires. `viewer/effects.js` now reads these words: the atlas is square, `columns = round(sqrt(frames))`, +1 if that undershoots (`0x0060a56a`–`c1`); the frame position advances `animationSpeed × ramp × dt / frames` per draw call (`0x0060a5c4`–`60e`), seeded from `initAnimationFrame` (`0x00609ea0`). **Still open:** wrap vs. clamp past the last frame — `draw`'s only render-record call (`RendPCDX8`+0x90, `0x00667830`, created this round) is point-size bucketing (`0x0062ce00`), not the UV/quad builder, so the wrap the viewer implements is untested against the real behaviour; all 29 checked vanilla templates advance ≤4.4 frames and never reach the strip's end either way |

## The proximity fuse and the round's lifetime (read 2026-09-24)

Why a flak shell bursts on the aircraft it passes, and when it bursts in the
sky. Everything below is lnxded, read from `Projectile::handleUpdate`
`0x0831e940` (Ghidra and objdump; the x87 compare directions are from the
disassembly at `0x0831ea44`..`0x0831ed5e`, not from Ghidra's rendering),
`Projectile::activate` `0x0831e120`, `Projectile::handleMessage` `0x0831e8f0`,
`Projectile::detonate` `0x0831e680` and the two console setters. Write-up:
[subsystems/projectiles-and-impacts.md](subsystems/projectiles-and-impacts.md)
section 5; viewer: `viewer/proximity-fuse.js`; build record
[`../flak-proximity-fuse/README.md`](../flak-proximity-fuse/README.md).

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| PROX-1 | **`explodeNearEnemyDistance` is `ProjectileTemplate+0x168` and `ProximityFusePrimer` is `+0x1a0`**; both default -1.0. The fuse runs only while `0 < distance` and the round is strictly older than the primer | **verified** | Setters: `ConsoleClass378::executeObjectMethod` `0x082dc5f0` writes `[getActiveTemplate(CID 0x86c2b90)+0x168]`, `ConsoleClass414` `0x082e4a30` writes `+0x1a0` (descriptor names at `0x086d5161` / `0x086d4fa1`, registered at `0x082ab350` / `0x082a9902`). Ctor `0x0831f8d0`: `param_1[0x5a]` and `[0x68]` = `0xbf800000`. Gates in `handleUpdate`: `fld [tmpl+0x168]; fucom 0; test ah,0x45; jne skip` (`0x0831ea4f`), then `mWorldTime - this+0x124` against `+0x1a0` with `jne skip` unless strictly greater (`0x0831ea62`..`0x0831ea7d`). `+0x124` is the launch time `activate` stamps from `mWorldTime` |
| PROX-2 | **The candidates**: `objectManager` vt+0x30 asked for objects within the distance of the round's position under `ObjectFlagPredicator(0x2000000)`; an object failing vt+0x28(`0xc4a4`) is skipped, a **soldier is skipped** (template class `0x9493` = `CID_BFSoldierTemplate`), one with no physics node logs and is skipped | **verified**; the flag's meaning, the vt+0x28 query and how the manager measures its radius are **not read** | `0x0831eab0`..`0x0831eb8f`; `cmp eax, ds:0x86c2b88` at `0x0831eb82` (`nm`: `CID_BFSoldierTemplate`). So flak never bursts on infantry and a man never sets off a landmine |
| PROX-3 | **In air** (the round's `getUnderWater` is 0): detonate on the first candidate that outweighs the round, weighs at most **100,000**, has its position within the distance (squared, to the object's position), and moves at **2.5 m/s or more** (`|v|^2 >= 6.25`) | **verified** | `PhysicsNode` vtable (`vt.py`): +0xa0 `getMass` (`0x0824d2e0`, `+0x6c`), +0xc4 `getUnderWater` (`0x0824d450`, a float at `+0x8c`), +0x38 `getPositionalSpeed`. `0x0831ec50`: own mass `<` other (`test ah,5; je skip`); `0x0831ec72`: other `<=` `ds:0x086c089c` = 100000.0; `0x0831ed1f`: `|d|^2 <=` distance squared; `0x0831ed56`..`0x0831ec3c`: `ds:0x086e1c58` = 6.25 `<= |v|^2`, then `call detonate` at `0x0831ec42`. The own mass is 0 when the round has no physics node (`0x0831ea90`) |
| PROX-4 | **Under water**: detonate on a candidate of mass **50,000 or more** whose height is within the distance (`|dy|` only) | **verified** | `0x0831ebcc`: `ds:0x086e1c5c` = 50000.0 `<=` other mass, then `fabs(this.y - other.y) <= distance` (`0x0831ec1c`..`0x0831ec3c`). The depth charge's branch; no speed test |
| PROX-5 | **No team is tested.** The loop resolves the firer's vehicle root and throws it away | **verified** | `playerManager` vt+0x18 on `this+0x108`, then vt+0x3c and `getRootParent` `0x0831ee3d`, whose result is never stored (`jmp 0x0831eb30`). A friendly aircraft crossing the stream sets a shell off; "enemy" is only the word's name |
| PROX-6 | **The proximity burst is the end-of-life burst.** `detonate` sets the latch `+0x10d`, calls `startEndEffect` (HP-9d's end-of-life explosion: `endEffectTemplate` plus the `damageType` 1/4 splash) and recycles the round | **verified** | `detonate` `0x0831e680`; the same function the expiry message calls (PROX-7). For the flak shells: `e_FlakBig`, material2 199, radius 20 |
| PROX-7 | **`timeToLive` is drawn per round** and posted as a timed message; on expiry a `hasOnTimeEffect` round detonates and any other is recycled with no burst | **verified** | `activate` `0x0831e120`: `getContinuousRandom(tmpl+0x17c, +0x180, +0x184)` (or the inline copy when `+0x188`), then `objectManager` vt+200 posts message `0x16` at that delay. `handleMessage` `0x0831e8f0`: on `0x16`, `tmpl+0x1a5` (`hasOnTimeEffect`, `makeScript` string `0x086e1d40`) picks `detonate` over `resetProjectile`. `AA_Allies_Projectile` `CRD_UNIFORM/0.8/1.4/0` at 300 m/s bursts 240..420 m out; `Flak38_Projectile` 0.8/1.2, 240..360 m. **Not modelled**: the viewer bursts every `damageType` 1/4 round at expiry whether or not it declares `hasOnTimeEffect` |
| PROX-8 | Vanilla's fuses: `AA_Allies_Projectile` and `Carrier_AA_Projectile` 10 m with `ProximityFusePrimer 0.1`; `Flak38_Projectile`, `AA_POW_Projectile`, `YamatoProjectile` 10 m; `LandmineProjectile` 3 m (mass 130); `FloatingMine` 3 m (mass 10000); `DepthCharge` 50 m (mass 800). XPack2 adds `FlakPanzerProjectile`; EoD has 17 | **confirmed** (data) | `Objects.rfa`, the `create Projectile` blocks under `Vehicles/Land/AA_Base`, `Land/Flak_38`, `Sea/Carrier_AA_Base`, `Sea/Common`, `Sea/Elco80`, `Sea/PrinceOW`, `Sea/Yamato`, `HandWeapons/Landmine`; `extract_map.py --damage-only` per mod |
| PROX-9 | A round that declares no `mass` is 1.0 | **verified** for the template; **inferred** for what its physics node's `getMass` returns | `mass` is `ConsoleClass92` (descriptor at `0x086c5874`), setter `0x081becc0` writing `SimpleObjectTemplate+0x54`; the ctor `0x081dbc70` writes `+0x54 = 1.0` (`0x081dbceb`) |

## Other formats

Add a section per format as it comes under investigation. Keep the same shape:
assumption, where it lives in our code, status, evidence.

| # | Assumption | Where | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BAF-1 | `.baf` stores transposed quaternions | [baf.py](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/baf.py) | **confirmed** (2026-09-16) | `BaseQuaternion<float>::toMat` (client 0x00461690, lnxded 0x08226180, both read) builds the ordinary unconjugated rotation from (x, y, z, w) — row 0 `1−s(y²+z²), s(xy−zw), s(xz+yw)` — with `s = 2/(x²+y²+z²+w²)`, so it needs no unit quaternion. `CompressedAnim::GetQuat` 0x0832fc90 feeds it channels 0–3 as stored, and `Skeleton::setRelativeBoneTransform` (client 0x0066b5c0) writes the result straight into the bone. `BaseMatrix4::mult` (client 0x0040d2e0) composes row-vector style (`v·M`, translation in the last row), and for this formula conjugating (x, y, z) gives exactly the transpose — so `baf.py`'s conjugate is the conversion to `ske.py`'s column-vector `_mul`, not a fix for bad data. Checked numerically on four clips at precision 15, 12, 11 and 11, to 7e-16. The same `s` makes the rotation immune to scale, which is why the engine survives `CompressedAnim::GetValue` dividing quaternions by `(1<<precision)−1` like every other channel: precision 10–14 clips decode 2–32× too long and still rotate correctly. **`baf.py` bug, fixed 2026-09-16:** positions were divided by `2^precision` (`baf.py:194`) where the engine divides by `2^precision − 1`; the reader now matches the engine. Quantified on the lowest-precision installed clip (GCMOD `sarlaac.baf`, precision 10): 19mm max / 1.9mm mean position delta removed, a 0.0978% relative error |
| TM-1 | A non-zero leading word that is not the collision magic is the visible vertex count | [treemesh.py:149-163](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/treemesh.py#L149) | **confirmed (fixed 2026-09-16)** | `TreeMeshTemplate::load` lnxded 0x083bd380 reads the word once (0x083bd651) and, when non-zero, passes it straight to `SmartItf<IVectorCollider>::create` as a class id (0x083bd85d); zero means no collider. The vertex array is read afterwards by `readBytes<VertexDifNormal2SetTex2D>`, which reads its own count, and nothing seeks back. The "collision magic" 0xEB97C2FA is `CID_SimpleCollisionMesh` (lnxded 0x086e9e08). Across 401 tree meshes: 126 zero, 275 that id, 0 anything else. `treemesh.py`'s dead rewind branch is now a raise on any id but `CID_SimpleCollisionMesh`/0; 401/401 installed tree meshes still parse clean |
| CON-1 | A bare `include <path>` line inside a `.con`/`.inc`/`.tweak` file can be safely skipped during model extraction | [extract_models.py](../../tools/bf1942-models/extract_models.py) | **refuted (fixed 2026-09-16)** | The archive-to-scene pipeline (`extract_models.build_library`) dropped every bare `include <path>` directive — 2,147 uses across 14 mods — instead of inlining it. `Objects/Soldiers/Common/CommonSoldierData.inc`'s `HitPoints 30` / `MaxHitPoints 30` / `objectTemplate.healFactor 0.25` / `selfHealFactor 0.15` / `repairFactor 0.15` never reached a `BFSoldier` template as a result, because every vanilla soldier kit reaches its hit points only through this include (see HP-11). Fixed via `extract_models._inline_includes`, resolving includes before the `.con` parse the same way the engine's own archive-open chain resolves a path (RFA-1) |
| RFA-1 | Archive names resolve case-insensitively, mod archives before parents' | [rfa.py](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/rfa.py) | **confirmed** (mount loop inferred) | Case: the `.rfa` file table is a map ordered by `NoCaseStringCompare`, i.e. `strcasecmp` (lnxded 0x08425af0), and `ZipArchive::findEntry` uses `equalsIgnoreCase` (0x08429ca0). Precedence: `FileManager::open` 0x0841dda0 tries a name's archives from the front and stops at the first that opens it, and `FileManager::addArchive` 0x0841f110 appends, so the earliest-mounted archive wins. `game.addModPath` is `Setup::addModPath` 0x080c8960, which appends in order, and every shipped `init.con` lists the mod before its parents. Not read: the call in `Setup::initFileSystem` 0x080bd8b0 that turns the path list into `addArchive` calls, so mod-over-parent and patch-over-base mount order remain inferred. Case matters in practice: 24,726 names occur with different case in different archives |

## Sound scripts (.ssc) — comments (settled 2026-09-16)

| # | Assumption in our code | Where | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SSC-1 | Comments are only `rem`, `//`, `***`, `;` lines; `/* */` is not a comment | [level.py](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/level.py) `_ssc_lines` | **refuted** (reader fixed 2026-09-16) | `SoundScript__skipFilter` 0x007f9a80 is the whole of it, and `/*` and `beginSkip` are **one** mechanism sharing the flag `g_sscSkipping` 0x00a8fb30. It runs *first* in the per-line pipeline `SoundScript__handleLine` 0x007fb0e0, ahead of `#templateLevel`, `#beginMap`, `#map`, `#include` and the 23-entry command table, so a skipped region swallows directives too. 849 of 52,310 shipped `.ssc` files across 17 mods use the markers |
| SSC-2 | A block comment is a character span, as in C | — | **refuted** | The filter tests whole lines (`operator==` for `beginSkip`/`endSkip`, `std::string::find` for the markers) and returns "consumed" for the entire line. So `/*load @ROOT/Sound/@RTD/bulletair3.wav` — XPack2 `Gewehr43_zf4/Sounds/High.ssc` — loses the `load` with the marker, and a self-contained `/* ... */` on one line never opens a skip at all, because the close test matches first |
| SSC-3 | An opener must be closed | — | **refuted** | There is no terminator requirement: the flag simply stays set. 373 of the 849 files never close one. Vanilla `Lynx/Sounds/High/LynxHorn.ssc` is a horn, then `/*`, then a whole copy-pasted Willy engine stack to EOF — read as live it made honking start a looping jeep engine (7 layers instead of 1) |
| SSC-4 | A `*/` with no opener is meaningful | — | **refuted** | The close test runs first and unconditionally, so it clears an already-clear flag and the line is dropped. Vanilla `KettenKrad/Sounds/High/KettenKradEngine.ssc` ships exactly this (an edit left it behind); the layers on both sides of it are live. 16 shipped files have a lone `*/` |
| SSC-5 | Skip state is per file | [level.py](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/level.py) `_ssc_lines` | **confirmed as global** | `g_sscSkipping` is cleared only by `SoundScript__resetParseState` 0x007fb040, called once at the end of `SoundScript__parse` 0x007fb1c0 — never per included file, since the same function also clears the accumulated patch list. The EOF test walks the include stream stack (0x00a8fc5c), so includes run through the same loop and the same flag: an unterminated `/*` inside an include keeps skipping in the includer |

### What it changed in our extracts

Measured over all 17 installed mods, comparing every `parse_ssc` consumer
before and after (49,980 hand-weapon / vehicle-engine / vehicle-gun decisions,
plus `discover_level_sounds` for the 23 published levels):

- **919 entries changed**, every one of them dead data that had been read as live.
- **Map ambience and area sounds: no change at all** (23 levels, 35 area sounds).
- **Vanilla hand weapons: no change** — every sample inside a `/* */` block in
  vanilla's hand-weapon data carries its own `Volume <- Time` ramp gating it to
  zero at the trigger, so `fire_sample`'s immediate-gain pick never chose one.
  Mods are not so lucky: XPack2's and WarFront's `Gewehr43` picked
  `sniperstereo.wav`, a sniper sample sitting inside a comment, where the live
  report is `k98lr.wav` / `gewehrshot.wav`.
- The three vanilla horns (Lynx, KettenKrad, BlackMedal) drop from 7 layers to
  1, and the Elco80 / Type38 PT boats lose a commented-out `Pitch <- Default`
  modulator on their bubble layer.

## Teams, factions and sides (`.con`) — all open, never opened the binary

Nothing below has been checked against the engine. These rows are here because
the assumptions are load-bearing for the viewer's browse facets and are stated
as fact in our code, which is exactly what this ledger is for. Every claim in
the Evidence column is from the shipped `.con` data, not from a function.

| # | Assumption in our code | Where | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SIDE-1 | A faction belongs to one of two sides, and the split is Axis vs Allied | [roster.py:100,176](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/roster.py#L100) `AXIS_NATIONS`, `side_of` | open | Never checked against the binary, and there is likely nothing to check: no symbol in [symbols.json](symbols.json) matches `team` at all. The level files know only numbers — `game.setTeamSkin <1\|2> <soldier>` in `Init.con` and `setObjectTemplate <1\|2> <vehicle>` in `Conquest/ObjectSpawnTemplates.con`. "Axis" and "Allied" are our words, mapped from a nation label we ourselves derived; the engine never names a side. `side_of` returns Allied for anything not listed, so a missing label is silently wrong rather than absent — which is how XPack2's whole elite-soldier line came out Allied until 2026-09-16 (`5d3594c`) |
| SIDE-2 | The two-sided WWII frame describes every mod | same | **refuted by the data** (2026-09-16) | Eve of Destruction has 18 kit-path nations and 14 team skins, and *every one* resolves to Allied — Viet Cong, NVA and Pathet Lao alongside ARVN, US, Navy SEALs and Special Forces. Both belligerents land on the same side, so the facet carries no information for that mod. [extract_kits.py:65-88](../../tools/bf1942-models/extract_kits.py#L65) already refuses the metaphor and returns no side outside a hardcoded WWII nation list, but `roster.py` — which is what `models.json` and the browse facets use — still forces the binary. The two disagree by construction |
| SIDE-3 | A template's nation is recoverable from kit-path and soldier-skin name tokens | [roster.py:33,113-114](../../tools/bf1942-models/bf42/roster.py#L33) `NATION_LABELS`, `KIT_SOURCE`, `SOLDIER_NATION` | open | A hand-maintained string table, so its failures are silent and per-mod. Unresolved tokens as of 2026-09-16: bf1918 loses its entire Central Powers side (`austrian_`, `bulgaria_`, `romania_`, `serbia_`, `turkish_`, `kuk`, plus `anz`, `bel`, `frz`, `senegal`, and near-misses `english_`, `rus_`, `japan_`, `usa`); FH `fmgerman`; DesertCombat `iraq`; FinnWars `finsummer`, `russummer`; EoD `aus` and four skins — `ARVNForces`, `AustralianForces`, `NavySeals`, `SpecialForces` — that `SOLDIER_NATION` cannot match because it requires the name to end in `soldier`. An unresolved token yields *no* faction, which is at least honest; only a resolved label can be mis-sided |

### Why SIDE-1 and SIDE-2 are worth a row rather than a fix

A team number is per level. The same vehicle is team 1 on one map and team 2 on
another, and XPack2's Essen spawns an identical vehicle list for *both* teams,
so a side cannot be attached to a template by reading one level. Our nation
table exists to give the browse grid a stable per-template answer, and that is
a product decision, not something the engine can settle.

The open question is therefore what to show, not what is true:

- Widening `AXIS_NATIONS` to cover the Vietnam communist factions would fix the
  split and print the word "Axis" beside a Viet Cong model.
- Returning `None` outside a known WWII roster — what `extract_kits.py` does —
  drops the facet for every non-WWII mod instead of lying about it.
- Naming the pair per mod (the level's own two teams) would be accurate and
  costs a schema change in `models.json` and the viewer's facet code at
  [index.html:4739,5331](../../tools/bf1942-models/viewer/index.html#L4739).

Until one is chosen, `roster.py` and `extract_kits.py` answer differently for
the same nation, and EoD's facet stays uninformative.

---

## Parse failures worth explaining (explained 2026-09-16)

Ten meshes across the installed mods fail to parse outright. None is a gap in
the reader. Eight are truncated files: every field up to the break decodes with
no slack, and the file ends inside the last thing it declares. The engine would
not reject them — `io::read` (lnxded 0x08417ae0) never checks how much it read —
so it loads whatever the stream leaves behind.

| File | Declares | Ends inside | Short by |
|---|---|---|---|
| GCMOD `Speeder_Fus_L1.sm` | 6 LODs | LOD 4's vertices | 8 bytes |
| WarFront `tri_mount_m1.sm` | 5 LODs | LOD 2's vertices | 3 bytes |
| WarFront `milif_short_pole_m1.sm` | 5 LODs | LOD 3's vertices | 240 bytes |
| bf1918 `MG08_Aim.sm` | 5 LODs | LOD 4's vertices | 331 bytes |
| GCMOD `reb_aa_top.sm` | 1 LOD | its indices | 2,094 bytes |
| DC_Final and DesertCombat `ah64_static.sm` (one file, two mods) | 1 LOD, 2 materials | the second material's vertices | 35,344 bytes |
| FinnWars `bengtskar.sm` | 1 collision layer | its BSP | 1,638 bytes |

The other two, bf1918 `standardMesh/e_Muzzle1_Sniper_m1.sm` and
`e_Muzzle3_Sniper_m1.sm`, are not StandardMesh data from byte 0: whitespace,
float-like bytes, and the file's own name at byte 59. What format they are is
open; the version check rejects them correctly.


---

## In-game HUD (2026-09-16; HUD-10, VHUD-9 and VHUD-11 closed 2026-09-19 and built 2026-09-20; VHUD-10's live writer found 2026-09-25; Ammo-HUD ownership stays open)

What paints the retail HUD: the soldier's health bar and stance figure, the
hand-weapon ammo panel, and the vehicle icon/health/seat-dot/ammo-panel group.
Settled for the map viewer's `hud.js`
(`features/bf1942-3d-models/in-game-hud.md`). Every number is bound through one
generic named-variable registry (`Soldier/*`, `Ammo/*`, `Vehicle/*`,
`CrossHair/*`, `Occupied/*`) that small per-group C++ objects populate once per
**rendered frame**, not the 30 Hz sim tick (HUD-3); drawing is a `TransformNode`
tree whose placement rule (HUD-8) and three fill-node classes (HUD-5..HUD-7) are
shared by every group, soldier and vehicle alike. Full narrative and every
address: [subsystems/ingame-hud.md](subsystems/ingame-hud.md).

**Extraction note (F1, 2026-09-16):** the vehicle icon word is `setVehicleIcon`,
applied only to a vehicle's root `PlayerControlObject`. No hand-weapon
equivalent exists — `addWeaponIcon` is Kit-only (a spawn-screen row), confirmed
absent from every `HandFireArms` across all 14 installed mods.

**Extraction note (F1, 2026-09-16):** the shipped data itself carries a
widespread typo, not a mod's: 13 of vanilla's 16 hand weapons ship
`setAmomBarPosX`/`Y` and `setAmomBarTextPosX`/`Y` instead of `setAmmoBar...`
(M1Garand, Type5 and JohnsonLMG use the correct spelling). Both spellings now
parse to the same fields.

### Soldier HUD: health bar, stance figure, hand-weapon ammo panel

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| HUD-1 | Named-variable binding is generic: typed registrars (int/bool/float/picture) wrap a live field pointer into the group's registry via a shared `register()` (`0x007ebab0`), gated on name+onCreate; the binder ctor is `0x007e9be0` | **confirmed** | Int/bool/float registrars `0x0069eb50`/`0x0069b810`/`0x006b3ae0`. The picture registrar (`0x0069b880`, corrected 2026-09-16) also takes a 3rd argument — the default-path field's own address — not just (name, group) as first read; its own ctor body (`0x007e9e20`) never reads that argument, so its exact role is unresolved |
| HUD-2 | `Soldier` HUD group (`registerVariables` `0x006e92a0`, ctor `0x006e95e0`, dtor `0x006e9640`) registers 7 names to raw fields at offsets +0x14/+0x18/+0x1c/+0x20/+0x24/+0x44/+0x64 | **confirmed** | Six of the seven are named directly: `SoldierHitPoints`, `SoldierMaxHitPoints`, `SoldierHealthBarIcon`, `SoldierHealthBarFullIcon`, `SoldierIcon`, `ShowSoldierIcon`. `SoldierBarSize`/`SoldierType` are never written by the client (0 hits, exhaustive string search) |
| HUD-3 | The Soldier HUD group updates once per **rendered frame**, not the 30 Hz tick | **confirmed** | `0x006ad0a0` has exactly 2 call sites, both from `Renderer_drawFrame` (`0x00466d80`). Pose/stance icon: `SoldierHud::setPoseAndIcon` (`0x006e9430`) when the current control object's template classID is `0x9493` (`CID_BFSoldierTemplate`), else `SoldierHud::reset` (`0x006e9570`) sets pose→0 and icon→cached fallback. Health-bar icons refresh separately via `SoldierHud::setHealthBarIcons` (`0x006e94b0`), gated on the player's own body independent of vehicle occupancy; its feeders (`0x006ac780`/`0x006ac760`) are unopened |
| HUD-4 | Stance = `(flags & 0x20) ? 1 : (flags & 0x40) >> 5` — 0 standing, 1 crouching, 2 lying | **confirmed** | `getSoldierPose`, `0x004f6ca0`; same formula family as movement-speed selection. The nation key that turns a pose into `Soldier/Icon_<nation>_soldier_<pose>.tga` is unread (open) |
| HUD-5 | The three HUD fill-node classes (`BfPictureFillNode`, `BfVariablePictureFillNode`, `BfVariablePictureFillNode2`) share one `draw()`, differ only in `update()` and plain-vs-`BfTransformNode`(OBJ)-bound fields | **confirmed** (strongest evidence: direct vtable memory reads) | `draw()` = `0x007e74c0` → `PictureNode::draw` `0x007ebfd0`, confirmed by **direct memory read of all three vtables' +0x54 slot**, not inference; 69 xrefs to `0x007ebfd0`. `update()` (vtable+0x68): `0x007dcb10`/`0x007dccf0`/`0x007dceb0` |
| HUD-6 | `Size` is a texel count along the fill axis: `visibleFraction = (Size / textureNativeDimension) × (Variable/Maximum)` | **confirmed** | Base helper `0x007dc610`; the Size-variant `0x007dc690` divides by the node's own `Size` instead of an external texture dimension. No `Maximum` binding collapses the fraction to 0 (not full) on both the horizontal and vertical paths |
| HUD-7 | Vertical `Fill order`: `false`=top-anchored/fills down, `true`=bottom-anchored/fills up; **has no effect on horizontal bars at all** | **confirmed** | `0x007dceb0`'s `update()`. The retail health-bar instance is data-confirmed `Fill order = True` (bottom-anchored, fills upward) — read directly out of `menu/InGame`, not inferred. The horizontal branch (`0x007dc710`) never tests the bit; the retail ammo bar's own `Fill order` (`False`) is moot |
| HUD-8 | `TransformNode::draw` (`0x007e92b0`) is the whole coordinate law: a child accumulates `parent.xy + local.xy` plus its own `w`/`h`; an untouched sibling reuses the parent's original rect | **confirmed** | Full **register-level disassembly**, not decompile alone (the decompiler mistypes a parameter here). An additional, context-gated branch exists (a 6th "context" parameter, a call to `0x007f46a0`, a sign-flip) and was not traced — confirmed irrelevant to every static HUD bar (rects reproduce to the pixel under the simple rule) but do not assume it covers 100% of `TransformNode`'s behaviour |
| HUD-9 | Soldier hand-weapon ammo panel (`AmmoType==1` branch): `BfVariablePictureFillNode2` bound to `SoldierAmmoBar`/`Fill`/`Ammo/PrimaryAmmo`/`Ammo/MaxPrimaryAmmo`/`SoldierAmmoBarSize`(20); `PrimaryAmmo` (rounds) top-right in `Style/InGameAmmoRightStyle`, `PrimaryMag` (spare mags) in a separate black-tinted box (`InGameMagCenterLatin`, `EffectNode`→`ColorEffect(0,0,0,1)`) | **confirmed by data** | Positioned via a `BfTransformNode`'s OBJ-bound `PosX`/`PosY` (data: `SoldierAmmoPosX=6`, `PosY=4294967279`=−17 as u32). Mag box gated visible by `SoldierAmmoHasMag`, hidden when `PrimaryMag == -1` (`4294967295` on the wire) |
| HUD-10 | `Ammo/AmmoType`'s `.meme` value and `setHudAmmoType`'s `.con` value are **the same enumeration**; no converter exists, and the row's "different enum" premise is refuted. **Seven** named values, not six: `ATNone=0, ATAmmoBar=1, ATIcon=2, ATIconAndStrengthBar=3, ATIconAndReloadBar=4, ATIconNoText=5, ATIconAndHeatBar=6`, anything unrecognised `=7`. The viewer should feed the weapon's own numeric value straight through; the one real change is `aticon` → **2**, not 6, which makes a bazooka show its rocket count. **`setHudAmmoType` is ALREADY parsed** by `bf42/con.py:1875-1883` (→ `hud_ammo_type`, emitted as `hudAmmo` at `con.py:1264`) and already consumed by `map.html:7609-7640`; the claim that it is a missing extractor word is false | **confirmed** (2026-09-19) | Client `operator>>` `0x004c4cd0` decompiled in full, every stored value read. The variable is an int at `AmmoHud+0xc` (`0x006e9f73`, name string `0x009261b4`); note that the weapon-icon registrar `0x006e8ca0` registers `NumberOfWeaponIcons` at `+0xc` of a *different* object in the same `"Ammo"` group. Layout gates read out of `hud-layout.json` / `menu/InGame`: three mutually exclusive soldier ammo-panel copies — `eq 1` (magazine panel + fill bar bound to `PrimaryAmmo`/`MaxPrimaryAmmo` + rounds + mag box); `∈ {2,3,4,5}` (panel + variable-picture icon, rounds text gated `ne 4 && ne 5 && ne 6`, `eq 3 →` heatbar art as the strength bar, `eq 4 →` reloadtimebar); `∈ {6,7}` (panel + icon, `eq 6 →` heatbar, `eq 7 →` reloadtimebar). So **`2` is tested**, as a member of the `{2,3,4,5}` group — an earlier reading that "0 and 2 are tested by nothing" was wrong — and there is a **dead `eq 6` leaf nested inside the `{2,3,4,5}` group** that can never fire. The shifted-by-one hypothesis is excluded because `.meme 1` draws a magazine panel and would have to be `ATNone`. Per-type feed: `ATNone` 0 (nothing draws; vanilla `KnifeAllies`/`KnifeAxis`), `ATAmmoBar` 1, `ATIcon` 2 (panel + icon + rounds, no bar — Bazooka, Panzerschreck, ExpPack, Detonator, Landmine), `ATIconAndStrengthBar` 3 (grenades), `ATIconAndReloadBar` 4 (RepairPack, no rounds), `ATIconNoText` 5, `ATIconAndHeatBar` 6 (MedPack). Survey across all archives of 18 installs: `ATAmmoBar` 1076, `ATIcon` 542, `ATIconAndStrengthBar` 379, `ATNone` 229, `ATIconAndHeatBar` 54, `ATIconAndReloadBar` 23, `ATIconNoText` 1; 37 declarations in vanilla. **Still open:** which slot of the `0x00a5f1a8` HUD singleton owns `AmmoHud` (`0x006e9f30` is reached only through the data vtable slot `0x9260fc`) |

### Vehicle and manned-gun HUD

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| VHUD-1 | One client constructor, `0x006d6500`, registers the whole `Vehicle/*` namespace plus `Occupied/OccupiedData`: `ShowVehicleIcon`/`ShowTurretIcon` (bool), `VehicleHitPoints`/`VehicleMaxHitPoints` (float, via `0x006b3ae0`), `VehicleIcon` (picture, handle cached at +0x54), `RedrawVehicleIcon` (bool), `VehiclePosX1..10`/`VehiclePosY1..10` (int, 10-slot arrays), `ShowVehiclePlayers` (bool), `VehiclePlayersText1..7` (string, 7-slot array) | **confirmed** | Vanilla's `menu/InGame` wires only 6 of each 10/7-slot capacity — slots 7–10 and text-7 exist and would work for a mod but are unused shipped (0 hits searching the file for them) |
| VHUD-2 | `Occupied/OccupiedData` (class `BfOccupiedVehicleData`, ctor `0x007dd8b0`) is **one shared object, not one per seat**; its 5-state icon table is `0` empty, `1` `Icon_vehicledot_local.tga`, `2` `Icon_vehicledot_empty.tga`, `3` `Icon_vehicledot_friend.tga`, `4` `Icon_vehicledot_enemy.tga` | **confirmed (table); open (live per-seat selector)** | Constructed once inside `0x006d6500`, registered under the group name `"Occupied"`; every `BfOccupiedVehicleNode` in the file references it by name. Vtable `0x0093f300` read directly as raw bytes gives the 5-slot table; which live state a given seat resolves to is unread |
| VHUD-3 | `Ammo/PrimaryAmmoBar`/`SecondaryAmmoBar` (`AmmoBar` enum) is fully decoded, 0–7: `None, AmmoBarOnly, AmmoBarHeatBar, AmmoBarReloadBar, HeatBarOnly, ReloadBarOnly, IconOnly, <anything else>`. The plain `.con` spelling `ABAmmoBar` (no suffix) silently falls to the same numeric default (7) as a typo — distinct from the *named* `IconOnly` (6); VHUD-4's own exclusion sets don't hide the count bar at 7 in either panel layout, so "icon-only" is not established for this fallback value | **confirmed** | `operator>>` `0x004c4ef0`; lnxded `setPrimaryAmmoBar`/`setSecondaryAmmoBar` `0x0831b710`/`0x0831b7d0`. `ABAmmoBar` used 520× across installed mods (survey re-run independently, byte-identical) |
| VHUD-4 | The ammo-count bar's own visibility test is asymmetric between panel layouts: the two-icon copy excludes bar values `{0,4,5}`, the single-icon copy (and the secondary weapon's copy) excludes `{0,4,5,6}` | **confirmed by data** | Read directly out of the raw `menu/InGame` dump at both sites |
| VHUD-5 | `CrossHair/CrossHairType` is `None=0, Icon=1, CrossHair=2, <anything else>=3`; typo'd spellings fall to 3. The entire crosshair region — plain crosshair **and** the sniper-scope overlay — is additionally gated on `NotData(Submarine/ShowPeriscope)` | **confirmed** | `operator>>` `0x004c5110`. Survey: `CHTNone` 8868, `CHTIcon` 2607, `CHTCrossHair` 1077, plus 44 typo'd spellings across 4 variants, all rendering as `None`. Periscope gate: `CullNode@83890` |
| VHUD-6 | A plain named `BoolData` gating a `CullNode` shows its content when the variable is currently **true**; the file's inline `[Value=…]` is only that variable's authored resting default, not "the value required to show" | **confirmed by data (self-consistency)** | 5 independent True-default/shown-by-default gates (`ShowWeaponIcon`, `ShowSoldierIcon`, `ShowCrossHair`, `Time/ShowTime`, `ControlPoint/ShowControlPoints`). No single instruction implementing `CullNode`'s own runtime test was located — only its file-reader, `meme_CullNode__read` `0x007f00d0`, exists in the corpus |
| VHUD-7 | Vehicle icon/health/seat-dots, the turret-turn dial, the vehicle-players tooltip and both ammo-panel layouts all have absolute 800×600 rects, independently re-walked and reproduced to the pixel | **confirmed** | Icon (`Vehicle/VehicleIcon`) fixed 128×128 native size at (200,462); health bar (32,64) at (174,525), gated `CullNode@57017`. Seat dots: six 8×8 dots at `(192+VehiclePosX[i+1], 452+VehiclePosY[i+1])`. Turret dial (also gated on `ShowTurretIcon`): back-plate 64×64@(400,540), pipe 16×32@(418,540), rotating body 32×32@(410,550). Vehicle-players popup: 220×85@(201,502), 6 rows at 15px pitch, auto-clears after `VehiclePlayersTimeOut` (3s). Ammo panel: 2-icon primary/secondary (600,514)/(720,514); 1-icon fallback (710,514) — same anchor as the 2-icon primary; soldier-skin panel (`NotData(Vehicle/ShowVehicleIcon)`) background at (589,525) |
| VHUD-8 | Every seat of a multi-PCO vehicle carries its own independent `Vehicle`/`Ammo` icon and bar words — naval AA mounts uniformly `ABIconOnly`, big naval guns `ABReloadBarOnly`, land/deck MGs `ABHeatBarOnly`, torpedo tubes `ABAmmoBarReloadBar` — **but hit points are not independent per seat** | **confirmed (icon/ammo); corrected (hitpoints)** | `hasArmor`/`hitpoints`/`maxhitpoints` are set exactly once, on a vehicle's **root** PlayerControlObject; a survey of 20+ companion seat PCOs across 11 vehicles (Fletcher, Hatsuzuki, PrinceOW, Yamato, Elco80, Type38, Lcvp, Daihatsu, Enterprise, Shokaku, Sherman) found zero that set their own hitpoints. One shared `Armor` backs every seat — the HUD's vehicle-health bar must bind to the vehicle root, not the occupied seat; switching seats within one vehicle never changes the displayed HP (see also HP-12) |
| VHUD-9 | What sets `Vehicle/ShowTurretIcon`, and what `IconLookRotation` measures — its unit, sign and pivot | **closed 2026-09-19.** `ShowTurretIcon = (seatCamera->getViewMode() == 3) && pcoTemplate->getHasTurretIcon()`. `IconLookRotation` (the layout's own name is unqualified, not `Vehicle/IconLookRotation` as this row and the prose used to say) `= atan2(dot(pcoRight, camForward), dot(pcoForward, camForward))` in **radians**, positive to the PCO's right, pivoted on the *controlled* PCO's own axes — the hull for a tank driver. `RotateEffect` applies it **counter-clockwise on screen**; HTML canvas `rotate(+θ)` is clockwise, so the two conventions are opposite. `angleMultiplier` scales a draw-context scalar, not the bound variable, and is `0` on all seven vanilla `RotateEffect`s, so leaving it unapplied is right | Both writes are in the per-frame HUD updater `FUN_006ad0a0` (HUD-3's function). Trigger, client `0x006ae597`–`0x006ae5d1`: `EBX = arg0`; `CALL [EBX_vt+0x34]` (getCameras) → `CALL [+0x14]` (getViewMode) → `CMP EAX,3`; if equal, `CALL [ESI_vt+0x60]` where `ESI = template->queryInterface(*0x008de480)` (set at `0x006ae46e`–`0x006ae47e`); `AL` → `[HUD_singleton[3] + 0xac]`. **`*0x008de480 = 0xc4c4 = IID_IPlayerControlObjectTemplate`** (lnxded names it at `0x086d3c4c`) — *not* the invented `IID_BFArmorOrHudAspect_c4a4` an earlier report proposed — and in `vtable for PlayerControlObjectTemplate` `0x0873eba0` (group `sym+0x204`, vptr `sym+0x20c`) `getHasTurretIcon()` sits at **vptr+0x60**, an instruction-for-instruction match; the same table puts `getVehicleIconPos()` at vptr+0x30 and `getCrossHairType()` at **vptr+0x68** (not the +0x78 the report claimed). Slot ids re-derived from bytes: IPCO vptr = `vtable for PlayerControlObject` `0x0873eec0` + **0x1b8**, `getCameras` +0x34; ICameraObject vptr = `vtable for Camera` `0x087209a0` + **0x150**, `getViewMode` +0x14. Angle, `0x006ae5d9`–`0x006ae616`: `dot(A,u)`, `FCHS`, `dot(B,u)`, `FPATAN` (`0x006ae612`), `FCHS`, `FSTP [EBP+0x320]` — rows identified at `0x006ad5df`, where the first `getAbsoluteTransformation` call is followed by `ADD EAX,0x20` into `[ESP+0x30..0x38]` (**row 2 = forward = B**), the second with no offset into `[ESP+0x3c..0x44]` (**row 0 = right = A**), the third with `+0x30` giving the translation. Sense: `0x007edbf0` computes `x' = x·c + y·s`, `y' = −x·s + y·c` about the pivot (`0x007edc09`–`0x007edc4b`), so `(0,−1)` at θ = +90° → `(−1,0)`: top → left, counter-clockwise on a y-down screen. Data: `setHasTurretIcon` on **7 distinct vanilla templates** (Sherman, Tiger, PanzerIV, T34, T34-85, M10, Chi-ha; 10 sites with the per-level Kasserine copies) and **4,195 declarations across 18 installs, 4,160 × `1` and 35 × `0`** (a narrower earlier survey undercounted at 2,739/33) — exactly the turreted tanks, never a casemate hull, and always on the **vehicle root PCO template**. Caveat: the client is MSVC and its vtable indices need not equal lnxded's gcc ones; `getHasTurretIcon` at +0x60 matched exactly, but `getCameras` at +0x34 is inference-by-shape (lnxded's returns a container needing two dereferences), so the *semantics* are solid and the *name* for the client slot is not. **Built 2026-09-20 and proved by pixels:** the dial's sign is now correct as a pair — `hud.js` rotates `−angle` and `map.html` feeds the un-negated engine angle — and the two builds were served side by side, each painting the value *its own* pipeline feeds, at +90°, −90°, **+45° and 180°** (the last two added because a symmetric sprite could hide a mirror at ±90). All four crops are **byte-identical PNGs**. The trigger now reproduces the engine's: Sherman cockpit 4,711 opaque texels, Sherman chase **0**, Sherman hull gunner **0**, Wespe gunner **0**, Stuka rear gunner **0**. `_drawPicture`'s global negation was checked against the only other rotated leaves, the `hitIndicator`'s seven static literals, which are authored in `RotateEffect`'s own sense — so negating is right for them too. Unreachable in vanilla and left alone: a template with `hasTurretIcon` but no yaw axis yields no dial, and all seven vanilla declarations are turreted tanks with a traverse |
| VHUD-10 | Whether a **driver's own** weapons feed the ammo panel's live values, and which weapon fills primary versus secondary when the root declares `NumberOfWeaponIcons 2` | **registrar tables closed 2026-09-19; the live writer found 2026-09-25.** The `Ammo` weapon-icon group registers **13** variables: `NumberOfWeaponIcons` +0xc, `PrimaryAmmoBar` +0x10, `PrimaryAmmoIcon` +0x14, `PrimaryAmmoText` +0x34, `RedrawPrimaryAmmo` +0x38, `SecondaryAmmo` +0x3c, `SecondaryAmmoBar` +0x40, **`SecondaryAmmoIcon` +0x44**, `SecondaryAmmoText` +0x64, `MaxSecondaryAmmo` +0x68, `RedrawSecondaryAmmo` +0x6c, **`UnlimitedPrimaryAmmo` +0x6d**, **`UnlimitedSecondaryAmmo` +0x6e** — the last two close `in-game-hud.md`'s separate "do the `Unlimited*` variables exist" item: they do. The `Overheat` group registers `OverHeat` float +0x8, `ShowRecover` **int** +0xc and `Recover` float +0x10 — and **`SniperSight` (bool +0x84) belongs to the `CrossHair` group**, registered by a different function, not to Overheat. Feeder `FUN_006d6af0` computes `f = heat > 0.1 ? heat/heatMax : (reload > 0.1 ? reload/reloadMax : 0)` and stores **`1 − f`** into `[this+0x14]+0x8` (selector 1) or `+0xc` (selector 2): inverted, heat wins, strict 0.1 dead band on the raw values. **Which group object `[this+0x14]` is remains UNVERIFIED, and "selector 2 writes the reload bar" is type-inconsistent** with the Overheat group's `int` at +0xc — do not record the destination names. **Writer found 2026-09-25:** client `FUN_006e9180(this=Ammo group, param_2=FireArm node, param_3=bool isPrimary)` is the true field writer, decompiled verbatim. `isPrimary` branch: `*(this+0x34) = local_4` (`PrimaryAmmoText`), `*(this+0x6d) = (local_4==-1 || iVar5==-1)` (`UnlimitedPrimaryAmmo`), then calls `FUN_006ea490(sum, iVar5, magCount-1)` (bar/fill helper, not decompiled). Else branch (secondary): `*(this+0x68)=iVar5` (`MaxSecondaryAmmo`), `*(this+0x3c)=sum` (`SecondaryAmmo`), `*(this+0x64)=local_4` (`SecondaryAmmoText`), `*(this+0x6e)=unlimited` (`UnlimitedSecondaryAmmo`). `local_4` is the first element of the node's mags vector; `sum` is that vector summed; `iVar5 = magSize(+0x394) * numOfMag(+0x3a0)` read off the node's stats sub-object at `[node+0x4c]`. Cross-checked against lnxded: `dice::ref2::world::FireArms::getAmmoInActiveMag()` (`0x0828cbe0`) returns exactly `*mags(this)` (= `local_4`), `FireArms::getTotalAmmo()` (`0x0828cb60`) sums the same mags vector with the identical `-1` unlimited sentinel (= `sum`) — so **`PrimaryAmmoText`/`SecondaryAmmoText` is the round count of the active magazine, not the reserve total**; the reserve total lands only in the non-text `SecondaryAmmo` field. `FUN_006e9180` is called from the loop inside the (still function-object-less in Ghidra, same as the registrars) block at client `0x006d7050`–`0x006d733d`, which also contains the previously-known sole caller of the heat/reload feeder (`0x006d71ad → FUN_006d6af0`, same `EDI`/`this`). That loop runs `EBP = 1..2` (stops at `EBP==3`, matching the `NumberOfWeaponIcons ≤ 2` cap), walking a node list rooted at a `FireArms` range obtained from the seat root passed into the function (`EBX` param → `queryInterface` → a `getFireArms()`-shaped call at `vt+0x58` filling a `[begin,end)` pair), filtered by a type-ID compare against `DAT_008dcd14`. **`isPrimary = (EBP==1)`: whichever FireArm node this list yields first is primary, the next is secondary** — ordinal position in the seat's own FireArms range, not weapon type or con-file field name. Since the range comes off the seat/`PlayerControlObject` passed in, this is the **occupied seat's own weapons** (the driver's, for the driver's seat) — confirms the driver's-own-weapons half of the question. Remaining open: the exact identity of the `DAT_008dcd14` type filter and of `FUN_006ea490` (the bar helper), and `[this+0x14]` in `FUN_006d6af0` (unrelated storage, still unverified) | Registrars re-read from raw bytes with their name strings (both functions have vanished from the Ghidra project): weapon-icon `0x006e8da4`/`8dd2`/`8e00`/`8e2e`/`8e5c`/`8e8a`/`8eb8`/`8ee6`/`8f14`/`8f42`/`8f70`/`8f9e`/**`8fcf`**, group string `"Ammo"`; overheat `0x006e9934`/`9971`/`999f`, group string `"Overheat"` (`0x00926038`); `SniperSight` at `0x006e9a98` inside the registrar at `0x006e9a60`, group string `"CrossHair"` (`0x008deab4`). Feeder `FUN_006d6af0` decompiled and confirmed verbatim; its single caller `0x006d71ad` passes `ECX = EDI` and also reads `[EDI+0xc]` as the weapon-icon group, so `EDI` is a container of group pointers and `[EDI+0x14]` is merely *some* group. The static half stands (VHUD-8): a Sherman's root feeds cannon + `ABAmmoBarReloadBar` and coax + `ABAmmoBarHeatBar`. **Correction carried:** lnxded `0x08290cd0` `FireArmsBundle::getAmmo` is **not** an accessor — its body is the inlined gcc `std::vector::erase(begin,end)` idiom on `this+0x110` (`memmove@plt` with n = 0 at `0x08290cf7`; `finish -= (end−begin) & ~3`) and it reports no count while destructively emptying the vector; `FireArmsBundle::getTotalAmmo` `0x08290d20` is the real reader (`call [vt+0x7c]` → `queryInterface(IID_IPlayerObject 0xc378, 0x086c2a60)` → walk `[ebx+0x54]`, compare `[ebx+0x4c]->getClassID()` against `ds:0x86c2b8c`, accumulate). Whether that body is ICF folding or a wrong corpus description stays open |
| VHUD-11 | Nothing in the extracted data carries the seat-occupancy dots' positions (`in-game-hud.md`: "live-bound per vehicle") | **refuted — the data is carried, just not parsed** (2026-09-19) | `ObjectTemplate.setVehicleIconPos <x>/<y>` is a `Vec2` on the **PlayerControlObject template**: the root and every seat PCO carry their own, giving that seat's dot position in the 128×128 vehicle-icon texture space, which is exactly what `Vehicle/VehiclePosX<n>`/`PosY<n>` feed. Setter `PlayerControlObjectTemplate::setVehicleIconPos(dice::ref2::Vec2 const&)` lnxded `0x0831b5f0`, `getVehicleIconPos()` `0x0831b620`, at `IPlayerControlObjectTemplate` vptr+0x2c/+0x30 (`vtable for PlayerControlObjectTemplate` `0x0873eba0`, group `sym+0x204`, vptr `sym+0x20c`). Survey of all 18 installs including level-embedded objects: **19,089 declarations across 17 mods** (vanilla 183 raw, 173 unique files; a narrower earlier count said 109/12,285), of which **19,085 are a single `x/y` token**, 2 space-separated and 2 empty. All values integers; vanilla range X 12…99, Y 43…120. Sherman root `54/103` and `shermanBrowning_PCO1` `32/61`; Yamato root `66/78`, Rear `32/57`, Left `57/62`, Right `37/73`; Hanomag root `39/75` plus five seat PCOs; Willy root `40/79`, passenger `21/84`. Geometry check against VHUD-7: the dots are drawn at `(192 + VehiclePosX[i+1], 452 + VehiclePosY[i+1])` and the icon is 128×128 at (200,462), so Sherman's `54/103` lands at (246, 555), inside the icon. ~~**`con.py` does not parse the word** (0 hits)~~ — **parsed and fed 2026-09-20.** `con.py` reads `setHasTurretIcon` and `setVehicleIconPos` into the `hud` extras, and `hud.js` feeds the six dots from them. Measured on a scratch re-extract: Hanomag from the root gives states `[1,2,2,2,2,2]` at 39/75, 40/65, 30/59, 41/55, 20/49, 31/45, and from the third seat `[2,2,1,2,2,2]`; Sherman gives 54/103 and 32/61. All land inside the 128×128 icon once anchored at `(192+x, 452+y)`. A single-player page reaches three of VHUD-2's five states (0, 1, 2); 3 and 4 need an occupant that does not exist. **One trap found and fixed in the same pass:** when the pair is unfed, falling back to the leaf's own rect draws a **placeholder** — that rect *is* the pair's authored default, a 5 px diagonal staircase from (247,457) — which would have painted a wrong six-seat layout on every vehicle in the shipped tree until the next re-extract (measured on Kasserine's Hanomag: 0 texels with the pairs fed, **66** with them deleted). An unplaced seat now reports VHUD-2 state 0 and draws nothing, per dot, so a half-re-extracted tree still places what it has |

### Crosshair hit indication (2026-09-24)

The four short diagonal marks that flash on the crosshair when the local player's round hits a soldier or a manned vehicle. Question and capture: the owner's 1280×720 recording `1790223308128.mp4` (Berlin, DP on foot). Full narrative: [subsystems/ingame-hud.md](subsystems/ingame-hud.md) §"Crosshair hit indication".

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| XHIT-1 | The marks are data: `menu/InGame`'s crosshair top carries a third branch, a `TransformNode` (390,290,20,20) holding `BfMultiplyColorEffect2{Alpha = CrossHair/HitIndicationTime}`, then `VariableColorEffect{CrossHairRed/256, CrossHairGreen/256, CrossHairBlue/256, CrossHairAlpha}`, then `RotateEffect{Angle 0.8, multiplier 0}`, then four empty-picture quads at (−3,−2,1,3), (18,−1,3,1), (−3,20,3,1), (19,19,1,3). Its only gates are the group's own `CrossHair/ShowCrossHair` and `NotData(Submarine/ShowPeriscope)`: no `CrossHairType`, no `ScopeIndex`, so it draws over a scope, an icon crosshair or none at all | **confirmed by data** | `CullNode@83882`, `@83890`, `TransformNode@83914`, effects `@83946/@83954/@83962`, quads `@83970..@83994` (vanilla). Survey of all 18 installs: one `BfMultiplyColorEffect2` per `menu/InGame`, always bound to `HitIndicationTime`, and the same geometry in 17 (bfheroes alone draws a 96×96 picture instead). The radio menu has its own, bound to `Radio/RadioAlpha` (`extract_radio.py`). `extract_hud_layout.py` used to lose both the rotation (`emit_picture` dropped it on empty-picture fills) and the alpha binding (`resolve_color` kept only the `1.0` default), which is why these leaves read as "four fill ticks gated only on ShowCrossHair" |

Copy in the HUD updater `FUN_006ad0a0` (HUD-3): `0x006aeba1 mov eax,[esp+0x228]` (arg 3, the local player, null-checked at entry `0x006ad0aa`), `fld [eax+0x1cc]`, `0x006aebb5 fstp [ebp+0x26c]` |

There is **no team comparison**: a friendly hit marks exactly like an enemy one, and the check runs before `calcDamage`, so friendly-fire settings do not suppress it | **confirmed** | lnxded `GameServer::giveDamage` `0x0814b2e0`: `cmp [setup+0x324],0` at `0x0814b388` (`game.serverHitIndication`, console object 558, `dice::bf::setup` `0x08716b64`); `js` on arg 6 at `0x0814b39a`; root walk with `IID_IPlayerControlObject 0xc4c5`; `getTeam()` = IPCO vptr+0x74 (`0x0831afd0`, returns `+0x170`); then `triggerSpecialGameEvent(1, attacker)` (GameServer vt+0x3ec) and `addBulletsGivingDamage`. ### Hit direction and the red wash (2026-09-24)

What the screen does when the local player is hurt: the whole frame washes red and, for seven of the eight directions, a red arc marks the side the damage came from. `hud.js` used to skip the full-screen quad as an "artifact". The owner's recording `1790223308128.mp4` (60 fps, a 2/3 crop of a 2560×1440 game) shows it. Full narrative: [subsystems/ingame-hud.md](subsystems/ingame-hud.md) §8.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| HFD-1 | Two `BfMenu` fields, written together and only by one setter, `BfMenu::setHitFromDir(byte dir, float alpha)`: `HitFromDirAlpha = alpha`, `HitFromDir = dir`. Nothing decays them. The setter has two callers: the listen server's own `_giveDamage` for its local player, and the `HitFromPosEvent` a remote client receives | **confirmed** | Registrar `FUN_006e10b0`: `HitFromDir` through the int binder `0x007e9be0` at `BfMenu+0x358` (`0x006e2166`–`0x006e2185`), `HitFromDirAlpha` through the float binder `0x007e4730` at `+0x360` (`0x006e21aa`–`0x006e21c3`), group `"HitFromDir"` (`0x00925438`). Setter `0x006acb60`: `mov [ecx+0x360],eax; mov [ecx+0x358],edx` (`dir` zero-extended), `ret 8`. Its only callers are `0x004b0a17` and `0x00494de1`. An exhaustive search of every instruction with a `+0x358]` or `+0x360]` operand finds no other writer of the pair: the other hits sit in unrelated classes |
| HFD-2 | The alpha is the damage as a share of the victim's own max HP, capped: `a = damage / armor.getMaxHitPoints()`, above 0.75 set to 0.75, below 0 set to 0. On the wire it is `byte = trunc(a × 255)` and the receiver multiplies by 1/255, so a remote client sees at most 191/255. The listen server's local player gets the float unquantised | **confirmed (both binaries)** | Client `_giveDamage` `0x004b0439`–`0x004b0473`: `armor->vt[0x14]` (getMaxHitPoints, HP-15), `fdivr [damage]`, compared with the double 0.75 at `0x008dbfa0`, then with 0.0. Byte: `fmul 255.0` (`0x008d1a70`) → `_ftol` `0x00804af0` (truncates). lnxded `GameServer::_giveDamage` `0x0814b870`: the same clamp (`0x0814ba06`–`0x0814ba23`), then `fldcw` with `ah = 0xc`, i.e. chop, at `0x0814ba3e`. Receiver `0x00494dc3`: `movzx edx,[esi+0xd]`, `fild`, `fmul 0.003921569` (`0x008d5bc8`). `HitFromPosEvent` (lnxded `0x0811e6c0`, type `0x3c`) serialises the octant in 3 bits (`+0xc`) and the alpha byte in 8 (`+0xd`) |
| HFD-3 | The octant: `d = normalize(Pos3 − victim.getPosition())`, a **3-D** direction (row 2 of `calcLookAtMatrix(victimPos, Pos3, up)`, the identity when `|d| < 1.1920929e-7`). With `f = d·forward` (victim row 2) and `r = d·right` (row 0): `f > 0.9238` → 1; `> 0.3826` → `r ≥ 0` ? 2 : 8; `> −0.3826` → `r ≥ 0` ? 3 : 7; `> −0.9238` → `r ≥ 0` ? 4 : 6; else 5. `HitFromDir = octant + 1`.
