## Supply depots (2026-09-16; SUP-15 closed and SUP-17 half-closed 2026-09-19)

Ammo boxes, medical lockers, land/airplane repair pads and mobile vehicle
depots are all one engine class, `SupplyDepot`/`SupplyDepotTemplate`, reused by
at least one mod as an area-damage trap. Settled for the map viewer's
`supply.js`/`armor.js`. Full narrative:
[subsystems/supply-depots.md](subsystems/supply-depots.md). All addresses
`bf1942_lnxded.static` (`nm -C`/`objdump`) unless marked client.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| SUP-1 | `SupplyDepotTemplate`'s ctors (`0x08324fa0`/`0x08325190`) lay out **twelve** vectors, not seven: 6 `ammoType` fields (id, amount-dup-A, amount-dup-B, rate, 1/rate, regen-rate from `+0x144`) and 6 `vehicleType` fields at the mirrored offsets from `+0x1a0`. Each "amount" is stored twice — a live copy and a **cap/regen-ceiling copy that is a real runtime clamp**, confirmed for both `ammoType` (`+0x15c`) and `vehicleType` (`+0x1b8`) | **confirmed** | `radius=2.0f` (`+0x138`), `team=0` (`+0x13c`), `workOnVehicles=0`/`workOnSoldiers=1` (`+0x140`/`+0x141`, bytes) defaults; `+0x198` left uninitialized by the ctor. Clamp confirmed inside `update()` (`0x08324150`, `0x8324258`-`826d`) |
| SUP-2 | `addAmmoType`'s int id (`0x08325380`) is **not** the client's `AmmoType`/`setHudAmmoType` enum (see HUD-10/VHUD-3) — it matches an unnamed tag at `FireArmsTemplate+0x238` | **confirmed** | `FireArms::getAmmoType()` `0x0828d4c0` reads the tag; no registered setter for it was found. `AmmoType`'s own 7-entry enum: `operator<<` `0x0829af50` |
| SUP-3 | `addVehicleType`'s name (`0x08325530`) resolves to a numeric id **once, at `SupplyDepot` construction** (`0x08322cf0`); an unresolved name silently becomes `0xFFFFFFFF` with no warning | **confirmed** | Via `objectTemplateManager` (`0x0871dc28`) vtable+0x1c = `getTemplate(std::string const&) const` (`0x081d5f10`), then the found template's own vtable+0x1c = `ObjectTemplate::getId() const` (`0x0816af60`) — both confirmed by static vtable dump |
| SUP-4 | `SupplyDepot::handleUpdate(float dt, unsigned)` (`0x083240f0`) **ignores both its own arguments** and self-throttles on real wall time against a per-instance, phase-randomised timestamp | **confirmed** | `dice::ref2::WorldPref::mWorldTime` (`0x087435a0`) minus `+0x154`, against threshold `+0x1e8` (default 0.5s, never overridden by any console word in any installed mod). `+0x154` seeded `(rand()%30)/30.0` at construction and in `reset()`; the ctor additionally (only there) gates this randomisation behind an always-true-on-the-dedicated-server `dice::bf::game`(`0x0870d918`)`->queryInterface(IID_IGameServer` `0x086b1cf8``)` check |
| SUP-5 | Eligibility gates are identical across the give/heal/repair action and the three `show*IconInMenu` menu predicates: alive with a body, not the depot's own root parent, `team == <the depot instance's own cached team> OR that value == 0`, inclusive 3-D distance `≤ radius` | **confirmed** | Team is read from the **instance**'s own `+0x108`, not the template (`showRepairIconInMenu` `0x083245d0` confirmed this directly) |
| SUP-6 | Depots never look at other depots — depots stack | **confirmed (absence, exhaustive)** | A full read of `update`/`workOnSoldiers`/`workOnVehicles`/`reloadAmmo`/`healSoldier`/`repairVehicle` finds zero references to the global depot map (`ObjectManager::getSupplyDepotMap`, `0x081a3440`) or any other cross-depot state |
| SUP-7 | Ammo is a per-type leaky bucket: a countdown decrements by real `dt`; when it goes negative, whole units = `trunc(-countdown × rate[i])`, and the countdown tops back up by `units/rate[i]`. The reserve regenerates `+= dt × regenRate[i]` (unless pinned at `-1.0` = unlimited) and is clamped to the authored cap | **confirmed** | `update()` `0x08324150`, fully instruction-traced. Countdown `+0x124[i]`, reserve `+0x10c[i]`, cap = the "amount dup B" field (SUP-1) |
| SUP-8 | `SupplyDepot::reloadAmmo(IPlayerObject*)` (`0x08323770`) computes `reserve[i] -= (wholeUnits[i] − FireArms::reloadAmmo's return value)` — the return value is **used**, not discarded. `FireArms::reloadAmmo`'s (`0x0828cfd0`) own per-magazine mechanism is materially more complex than "count of whole magazines, echo the input": magazine 0 (active) is excluded from the top-up, overflow cascades to the next magazine, and a concrete zero-return path exists | **confirmed (consumption); open (exact per-magazine formula)** | Reserve genuinely depletes in real play; needs a follow-up pass to fully close the fill semantics |
| SUP-9 | Refill-sound triggering is **two independent mechanisms** | **confirmed** | `FireArms::checkIfToTriggerRefillSound()` (`0x0828fb40`) and its `HandFireArms` override (`0x08294480`) both gate on "current ammo increased since last check"; only the `HandFireArms` override additionally resolves a `BFPlayer` and, if `dice::bf::game->queryInterface(IID_IGameServer)` succeeds, broadcasts via that interface's vtable+0x2d4. `BFSoldier::triggerRefillAmmoSound()` (`0x0827ebc0`) is unrelated — no `IGameServer` reference at all, requires an emitter (`+0xf8`) and a per-weapon handle (`+0x4f8`) both non-null with no fallback, dispatches through the handle's own vtable+0xc |
| SUP-10 | `healSoldier(IPlayerObject*)` (`0x083239d0`), unlimited case (`this+0x130 == -1`, exact equality): heals/damages every call by exactly `-(this+0x14c)` (the per-cycle `dt × rate`), dispatched via a generic vtable+0xd8 **on the target player itself** | **confirmed (unlimited case); open (exact finite-budget clamp)** | A finite shared per-cycle budget path exists (`this+0x130` consulted, decremented) but moot in shipped content — no vanilla or mod template uses a finite positive heal budget |
| SUP-11 | The sign of `setHealth`'s rate argument selects heal vs. damage identically for soldiers and vehicles | **confirmed** | Vanilla mediclocker-style templates: positive rate (heal). FH's `AlliesKiller<n>m`/`AxisKiller<n>m` traps: `setHealth -1 -4.0 0`, team-gated `workOnSoldiers 1 workOnVehicles 1` — continuous damage. Each carries ~224–227 `addVehicleType <vehicle> -1 -10 0` lines; similar unmentioned kill-traps: DC_Final/DesertCombat `flagboxKill`/`IS_Kill`/`USS_Kill`, FH `PushCageKillInside`/`PushCage2KillInside` |
| SUP-12 | `isHealing()` (`0x08324540`) is a strict `rate > 0.0` test, distinct from the coarser "heal enabled this cycle" transient flag (`+0x152`, `rate != 0.0`, either sign) | **confirmed** | A damage-rate template is "healing-enabled" by the transient flag but not by `isHealing()` |
| SUP-13 | `isRepairing()`/`isReloading()` (`0x08324570`/`0x083245a0`) report **static template capability**, not transient state | **confirmed** | `count(vehicleTypes)!=0`/`count(ammoTypes)!=0`, computed fresh in both the ctor and `reset()` (`0x08324e40`) from the live vector length |
| SUP-14 | Vehicle repair/rearm has no per-type pacing timer; **almost every shipped template** uses only the unlimited reserve case, but not universally | **confirmed** | Grepped `repairVehicle`/`workOnVehicles` for any countdown vector: 0 hits — `repairVehicle` (`0x08323ba0`) applies its rate directly every call when unlimited. Exceptions that genuinely reach the finite-reserve/clamp branch: FHSW's `LST-1_MovableRampKiller`/`LST-1_StaticRampKiller` (`a2=-100000`, single-shot ramp traps), `R-11FM_Rocket_Selfrepair`/`Selfkiller` (`a2=50`/`25`). Do not assume every `addVehicleType` reserve is `-1` once a mod's content is in play |
| SUP-15 | `healDistance`/`healFactor`/`selfHealFactor`/`repairDistance`/`repairFactor` (`Objects/Soldiers/Common/CommonSoldierData.inc`) are real registered console properties — but **no consuming method exists anywhere in `SupplyDepot`/`SupplyDepotTemplate`** | **closed 2026-09-19: they are `BFSoldierTemplate` properties, consumed by `BFSoldier::useMedPack()` and `BFSoldier::useRepairPack()`** — the medic pack and the wrench, never by `SupplyDepot`, which is why the exhaustive SupplyDepot read found nothing. Distances are the object-sweep radius and the per-candidate range gate; the factors are HP **per invocation**. **The "≈7.5 HP/s at 30 Hz" figure the research pass derived is UNVERIFIED and must not ship** — it assumes the message is posted once per simulation tick while the pack is held, and that the tick rate applies here; neither was read | Offsets by accessor, each through `getActiveTemplate(CID_BFSoldierTemplate 0x86c2b88)`: `healFactor +0x2d8` (`0x082bc7b0`), `selfHealFactor +0x2dc` (`0x082bcbc0`), `repairFactor +0x2e0` (`0x082bcfd0`), `repairDistance +0x2ec` (`0x082bd3e0`), `healDistance +0x2f4` (`0x082bdc00`); ctor defaults 0.1/0.1/0.1/3.0/5.0 at `0x0827a35e`–`0x0827a3a4`. `useMedPack` `0x082768d0`: requires `this+0x3e8 != 0`, sweeps `objectManager->vtable[0x30](getPos(), healDistance, &out, ObjectFlagPredicator(0x2000000))` at `0x08276949` (the same spatial query `handleExplosion` uses), then per candidate of the same template id `distanceSqr <= healDistance²` (`0x08276bd0`), Armor present, `!isDestroyed()`, `hitPoints < maxHitPoints` (`0x08276c4e`), then `target->vtable[0xd8](−healFactor)` at `0x08276c6f` (sign flip `xor eax,0x80000000` at `0x08276c68`; HP-7 routes a negative through `Armor::heal`); self-heal fallback `0x082769c4`/`0x08276a05`; the target's HP fraction is cached into `self+0x204`. `useRepairPack` `0x08276100`: sweep radius `max(repairDistance, template+0x2f0)` (`0x0827613d`–`0x08276158`), per-candidate gate `distanceSqr <= (candidate->vtable[0x48]() + repairDistance)²` (`0x08276762`–`0x0827678a`) so a big hull is reachable from its skin, then `Armor::heal(repairFactor)` directly (vtable `+0x24`, `0x082761f3`). Both are called from `BFSoldier::handleMessage` at `0x08277720` / `0x08277796`, each behind an ammo test (`cmp [edx],eax; jle skip`). Vanilla `CommonSoldierData.inc`: `healFactor 0.25`, `selfHealFactor 0.15`, `repairFactor 0.15`, `repairDistance 2.0`, `healDistance 10.0` |
| SUP-16 | Team convention: `AlliedAirplaneSupplyDepot` is always `team=2`, `AxisAirplaneSupplyDepot` always `team=1` | **confirmed (code rule); inferred (numeric↔faction mapping)** | Confirmed by data across all installed mods, combined with SUP-5's exact-match-or-zero rule: **0 = both teams/neutral, 1 = Axis, 2 = Allied** (the Axis/Allied↔1/2 naming is from convention, not a labelled engine constant) |
| SUP-17 | The client HUD draw path that lights up the ammo/heal/repair icon in the 3D world (as opposed to the menu-prompt predicates of SUP-5) was not located | **half closed 2026-09-19: the variables are named, their per-frame writers are not** — and the naming half was **not independently re-derived** (the verifier spent its budget elsewhere), so treat it as the research pass's reading | **Client** `BF1942.exe`. The three in-world icons are booleans in the in-game HUD's named-variable registry, registered back to back on consecutive members of one HUD object: `ShowRepairIcon` (name string `0x00925940`, member `esi+0x2b`, registration `0x006e3174`–`0x006e3193`), `ShowHealIcon` (`0x00925930`, `esi+0x2c`, `0x006e31bb`–`0x006e31da`), `ShowReloadIcon` (`0x00925920`, next slot, from `0x006e3202`). Registrar is the generic bool one, `0x0069b810` (HUD-1), called with `this = [esi+0x4]`, `lea edx,[esi+0x2b]` and two `std::string`s built/destroyed through `ds:0x008c3134` / `ds:0x008c311c`; the group-name literal is `0x008c3846`. The neighbouring strings in the same block — `ShowControlPoints`, `ShowNonTakeableFlagIcon`, `ShowFlagIcon`, `CameraPlaced`, `EngineerNeeded`, `MedicNeeded`, `ShowParachute` — identify it as the world-icon group, and the same block holds VHUD-7's `IconLookRotation` site (`0x006e2013`). The three names map one-for-one onto `SupplyDepot`'s `show*IconInMenu` predicates (`showRepairIconInMenu` `0x083245d0` et al.), so SUP-5's eligibility rule is what they surface. **Still open:** who writes `+0x2b`/`+0x2c`/`+0x2d` each frame, and the identity of the registrar's owning object |

---

## Hit points and damage (2026-09-16, extended 2026-09-17; explosion falloff and fall damage closed 2026-09-19; the contact-recycle rule, the blast centre and DMG-2 added 2026-09-20)

Every Armor-bearing thing — soldier, vehicle, gun — has a `hitPoints`/
`maxHitPoints` pair that floors at 0 and dies there; death is a threshold
crossing, not a per-frame poll. Settled for the map viewer's `armor.js` and the
soldier's HUD health bar (HUD-9). **Collisions: HP-6 ("a collision never costs hit points") was closed in
the negative on 2026-09-17, refuted for a falling soldier on 2026-09-18 and
refuted outright on 2026-09-19 — the handlers call `GameServer::giveDamage`, so
vehicles are damaged by ramming and by crashing. See the HP-6 row, the
"Rigid-body collisions" section (COL-2...) and
[subsystems/collision-response.md](subsystems/collision-response.md) §9.**
Full narrative:
[subsystems/hitpoints-and-damage.md](subsystems/hitpoints-and-damage.md). All
addresses `bf1942_lnxded.static` unless marked client. Armor's vtable
(`0x0871d220`) was read in full (82 slots) and every `call [reg+N]` below is
cross-checked against that dump, not slot arithmetic.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| HP-1 | Clamp behaviour: floor snaps to exactly 0.0 below 0.001; `setMaxHitPoints` ceilings at 128.0 and never touches `hitPoints`; `setHitPoints` can raise but never lowers `maxHitPoints`; `heal` clamps to the current max; `damage` has no upper reference | **confirmed** | `setHitPoints` `0x081726c0` (epsilon `0x086c0308`=0.0010000000474974513); `setMaxHitPoints` `0x08173680` (ceiling `0x086c1fe8`=128.0); `damage`/`heal` `0x08172730`/`0x081727a0`, both gated by `dice::bf::game->queryInterface(IID_IGameServer)` (`0x0870d918`/`0x086b1cf8`) and by `isDestroyed` unless `canBeRepairedAndDestroyed` (`+0x12a`) is set — `damage()` ignores that flag entirely |
| HP-2 | Death is a threshold crossing at `pendingHP ≤ 0.001` inside `Armor::status(float)`, called by all three mutators before they commit | **confirmed** | `0x081739e0`: sets `isDestroyed`+`isCriticalDamaged`, resolves `lastHitPlayer` (`+0x14`) to a `BFPlayer*` (`0x0871dc2c` vtable+0x18 → `dice::bf::getBFPlayer` `0x08052ac0`) and, only when the dying template is **not** `CID_BFSoldierTemplate`, calls `BFPlayer::addVehicleDestroyed(unsigned)` (`0x08054770`) on the killer; then fires the dying object's own explosion if `explosionDamage > 0.001` (HP-8) |
| HP-3 | Spawn-time `Armor` construction has no separate template class: `setArmorComponent` copies **23 setter calls**, in a fixed order, into a freshly pooled Armor, `setMaxHitPoints` strictly before `setHitPoints`. **`material` is not among the copied fields** — Armor's material is always the constructor's hardcoded 0, regardless of any per-template value a `.con` file specifies | **confirmed (order, including the material omission)** | `SimpleObjectTemplate::setArmorComponent` `0x081ddae0`; pool `0x0871d210` (464 bytes/instance); ctor `0x08172280` hardcodes `+0x40=0` |
| HP-4 | Console property names case-registered once (camelCase); vanilla ships both that spelling and PascalCase and both work; lookup is case-insensitive in practice, exact compare function not isolated | **working** | `hitPoints`/`maxHitPoints` vs. `Stationary_Browning`'s `HasArmor`/`HitPoints`/`MaxHitPoints`, same archive as `Sherman`/`Defgun`'s lowercase. Compare routine (`~0x081b2ce0`) not isolated |
| HP-5 | Per-tick/per-second cadence in `update`: water contact recomputed every call; critical/upside-down loss gated behind a `+=dt` accumulator firing only once ≥1.0s accumulates (soldiers excluded entirely); water/deep-water use independent countdowns, first delay honoured, every fire after is a flat 1.0s | **confirmed** | `Armor::update(float dt)` `0x08172f40`; water-manager global `0x087435f0`; accumulator `+0xe8`; water/deep-water countdowns `+0xe4`/`+0x13c` live, `+0xec`/`+0x140` configured; flat loss (`hpLostWhileDamageFromWater`/`FromDeepWater`), not scaled by dt |
| HP-6 | ~~A collision never costs hit points~~ — **a collision costs hit points, for every armoured object, against other objects and against the ground** | **refuted twice** (2026-09-18 for a falling soldier; 2026-09-19 for everything, researched and independently re-derived from `objdump`, V0) | Both collision handlers end in `call *0x15c` on **`this`, the GameServer** (`handleCollisionObjectVsObject`: `mov 0x8(%ebp),%edx; mov (%edx),%ebx; ... call *0x15c(%ebx)` at `0x08155875`–`0x081558d6`), and slot `+0x15c` of the GameServer vtable (`0x0871b0e0`, vptr = symbol + 8) is `GameServer::giveDamage(IObject*, float, int, int, int, Pos3, int, bool, bool)` `0x0814b2e0`. The 2026-09-17 pass enumerated Armor slots and direct calls and so never saw it; the 2026-09-18 pass saw the call but read the receiver as the colliding object and the slot as `BFSoldier::handleDamage`, which kept the claim alive for vehicles. What stands from both: neither handler calls Armor `+0x20`/`+0x24` directly, `Game::playCollisionEffect` `0x0805de20` is a harmless leaf, and the client has no code caller of `Armor::damage` (hit points are server-authoritative). Formulas: COL-3, COL-4; narrative [subsystems/collision-response.md](subsystems/collision-response.md) §9 |
| HP-6b | The gate at `0x81dae30` is **self-collision suppression, not an `otherObject` type test** — and it is two chained comparisons, not one | **confirmed (corrects the earlier reading twice over)** | `0x81dad43`–`0x81dafc5`. Walks `getRootParent(this)` for `IID_ICompositeObject` (`0x86c2a58`) toward the nearest `IID_IPlayerControlObject` (`0x86d3c50`) ancestor; the `vtable+0x90` call is on *that* object, not on `otherObject`. It then compares `ObjectSpawner::getHoldObjectId()` (`0x08314a50`, via `objectManager` `0x0871dc24`) against **self's** own root (`edi+0x48`), and only if that matches does an `ICompositeObject`-identity compare against `getRootParent(otherObject)`. The bypass (`jmp 81daea6`) fires only when both pass — a shell does not record a collision against the gun that fired it |
| HP-6c | Terrain and water contact route through the **same** dispatcher as object-vs-object, with `otherObject` forced null. There is no separate physics-only terrain-damage path to look for | **confirmed** | `GameServer::handleCollision` `0x08156020`, 101 bytes: a tail-call (`jmp`) dispatcher, `mov [ebp+0xc],0x0` before the LandOrWater jump. Reached through the `dice::bf::game` global's own vtable (`0x0870d918`), slots dump+0x38/+0x3c, selected by whether `this`'s `+0x4c` class is `CID_ProjectileTemplate` (`0x86c2b90`); the singleton is a `GameServer`, whose vtable (`0x0871b0e0`) overrides both slots away from `Game`'s base implementations (`0x0870d760` → `0x0805f640`/`0x0805f740`) |
| HP-6d | `setLastCollisionHeight` stores the colliding object's **raw world Y at the moment of contact**, not a derived fall distance, and it is read in exactly one place | **confirmed** | Write site `0x81dae83`–`0x81dae98` in `SimpleObject::handleCollision`: `this->vtable[0x38]()` returns a Pos3, its `+0x4` (Y) is passed to `Armor` vtable+0xf8. A whole-binary scan of all 32 `+0xf8]` call sites found this to be the only one on an Armor. Read back at `0x08154d37` (`[eax+0xfc]`) in `handleCollisionLandOrWater`'s `CID_BFSoldierTemplate` branch. `Armor+0x28`, a plain float |
| HP-7 | `SimpleObject::handleDamage(float amt)` is a **find-nearest-Armor-then-dispatch-by-sign** function, not a parent-escalation dispatcher | **confirmed (mechanism corrected)** | `0x081db230`: walks the composite chain (self, then `+0x50` repeatedly) for the nearest ancestor exposing an Armor component (`getComponent(0xc4a4,0xc4a4)`); dispatches by the sign of `amt` against literal `0.0`: `amt>0.0` → that Armor's `damage(amt)` (vtable+0x20); `amt≤0.0` → the **same** Armor's `heal(-amt)` (vtable+0x24), negation via XORing the sign bit of the `amt` float parameter itself — not a "collision-flags byte". No per-body-part or per-material scaling here |
| HP-8 | Splash damage always uses the exploding/dying object's own `explosion*` fields, self-excluding the source by Armor-pointer identity | **confirmed, with an open caveat** | `GameServer::handleExplosion` `0x08156ef0` sweeps a spatial index, calls `handleExplosionOnObject` `0x08156500` per target. An object's own on-death explosion (fired from `status()`) passes `sourceArmor=NULL`, so this pointer-identity exclusion is inert on that specific path — how it avoids self-damage there is unresolved |
| HP-9 | Vehicle/gun explosion: hard cutoff at the configured `explosionRadius` + a material-pair multiplier feeding `calcDamage`; exact within-radius falloff shape unresolved | **confirmed — closed 2026-09-19.** Explosion falloff is **linear**, `t = clamp((radius − d)·(1/radius), 0, 1) = 1 − d/radius`, times damage, times `getDamageMod(explosionMaterial, victim.material)`, times soldier exposure (1.0 otherwise), then `calcDamage` friendly-fire scaling. Distance is to the victim's `getPos()` **origin** with only the Y term scaled by `YModOnExplosion`; **no occlusion at all for non-soldiers**. Impact explosion requires `damageType == 1` **and** `hasCollisionEffect`; the end-of-life explosion requires `damageType ∈ {1,4}` and tests neither `hasCollisionEffect` nor the truncation. `radius` defaults to **10.0** in the constructor and is a console **`int`** — a `.con` cannot give a projectile a fractional radius | `handleExplosionOnObject` `0x08156500`: distance `0x081565c2`–`0x08156646` (`getPos` is IObject vtable `+0x38`, called at `0x081565c2`; only `dy` multiplied, `0x08156613`), cutoff `0x08156655`/`0x0815665b` (`fucom st(1)` + `test ah,0x45`, continue only when `radius > d`), falloff `0x08156664`–`0x0815668f`, `getDamageMod` `0x081565b4` (MaterialManager vtable `+0x4c`), exposure `edi` seeded `1.0f` at `0x08156505` and replaced from `checkForHitOnSoldier` `0x08156eb6` at `0x08156ece` (exposure `== 0.0` short-circuits at `0x08156ede`), folded in at `0x081566b4`, `calcDamage` `0x081566c4`. `A = 1/radius` is computed once by `handleExplosion` `0x08156ef0` at `0x08156f5c` (`fld radius; fdivr ds:0x86ba8d4`, `0x086ba8d4` = `00 00 80 3f` = 1.0f) and pushed at **both** call sites, `0x081570e4`/`0x081570ed` and `0x08157262`/`0x08157271` — so both clamps are dead given the strict gate. `handleExplosion` is `game` vtable call-offset `+0x38` (read from the real table: `+0x30` handleCollisionForProjectile, `+0x34` handleCollision, `+0x38` `0x08156ef0`). Properties recovered per `ConsoleClass` instance (`+0x0c` name, `+0x4c` declared type) on `CID_ProjectileTemplate` `0x86c2b90`: `damageType` `+0x160` int (accessor `0x082dc1e0`, gate `0x08153e79`), `radius` `+0x190` **int** (accessor `0x082df8b0`, parser `istream >> int` `0x082df83f`, `fild` into the float field `0x082df8ef`, ctor default `0x41200000` at `0x0831f9b3`), `hasCollisionEffect` `+0x1a4` bool (accessor `0x082dd7d0`, gate `0x08153ea9`, and two further pairs at `0x0815428e`/`0x0815429b` and `0x08154600`/`0x08154631`), `forceOnExplosion` `+0x1b0` float, `YModOnExplosion` `+0x1b4` float, `material2` `+0x1b8` int. End-of-life `Projectile::startEndEffect` `0x0831f590` takes `== 1` at `0x0831f6bb` and `== 4` at `0x0831f6c0`, pushes `sourceArmor = 0` at `0x0831f727` and an **untruncated** radius at `0x0831f73e`. Victim material is `SimpleObject+0xe8`, copied from `SimpleObjectTemplate+0x88` = `ObjectTemplate.material` (`0x081da0ff`; accessor `0x081c1410`). The impact-path truncation at `0x08153f23`–`0x08153f58` is real code but a **no-op in practice** because the property is an int — and the 340 mod templates that write a fractional radius are truncated at parse: FH `BismarckFatProjectile 17.63` → 17, DC `50calSniper_Projectile 0.25` → **0**, which with the `radius > d` gate means no splash at all. **Blast centre, added 2026-09-19: the impact explosion is centred at `hitPos + 0.1 · normal`, not at the hit point** — `0.1f` at `ds:0x086b1ca0` (`cd cc cc 3d`) loaded `0x08153f5e`, multiplied into the normal `0x08153f6b`–`0x08153f73`, added `0x08153f82`–`0x08153f8f` and pushed `0x08154026`/`0x08154030`/`0x08154037`, with the same shape at `0x0815434e` and `0x081546f2`. The collision **effect** is played earlier, at `0x08153e5b`, on the raw unoffset point, and the end-of-life explosion has no offset at all (`startEndEffect` pushes `getPos()` at `0x0831f747`). Worth about 1% of the falloff at a typical radius — free to model and now read. **Parse-time confirmation of the int, 2026-09-19:** `ConsoleClass390::setArgFromString` `0x082df7b0` builds a `basic_stringbuf` over the argument, runs `std::istream::operator>>(int&)` `0x082df83f` into a function-local static at `0x087debbc`, and **returns void having never consulted the stream state** — no `fail()`, no `rdstate()`, no branch on the result. `ConsoleObjectBaseImpl::execute` `0x08359460` ignores the return and unconditionally calls `executeObjectMethod` `0x082df8b0`, which `fild`s that int into `ProjectileTemplate+0x190` (`0x082df8ef`/`0x082df8f5`). There is no rejection path: `>> int` takes the `0`, stops at the `.` and sets no failbit, so `0.25` really is 0 and `7.5` is 7 |
| HP-9b | `handleExplosionOnObject` **queues** damage rather than applying it: on `calcDamage > 0` it appends {objectId, damage, E, F, G, Pos3} to six double-buffered `std::vector`s on the GameServer; its bool return only feeds the shot-accuracy stat | **confirmed (the queue); open (its consumer)** | Appends at `0x081567b9`–`0x0815690a` to `GameServer + [this+0x2b4]*12 + 0x224/0x23c/0x254/0x26c/0x284/0x29c`. Return computed at `0x08156767`–`0x08156781` (the victim PCO's `getTeam()` vs the explosion's team), consumed by `handleExplosion` at `0x081570f5` to gate `BFPlayer::addBulletsGivingDamage` `0x08156fb5`. No reader of `+0x224` identified; **not independently re-derived** by the verifier |
| HP-9c | `GameServer::calcDamage` `0x0814b520` is **friendly-fire scaling only** — no material, no distance. It returns the damage untouched unless the team argument is 1 or 2, then multiplies by `GameServer+0x2c8`/`+0x2cc` (soldier/vehicle, non-splash) or `+0x2d0`/`+0x2d4` (splash). Splash always passes the splash bool; a vehicle's on-death explosion passes team `−1` and so is never scaled | **confirmed (structure); the four offsets not individually re-derived** — **re-derived 2026-09-24 with their setters and console words, and the early-out named (god mode): FF-3** | Team gate `0x0814b542`–`0x0814b54c` (`dec eax; cmp eax,1; ja return`), `CID_BFSoldierTemplate` split `0x0814b604`/`0x0814b700`, `getTeam` via PCO vtable `+0x74` at `0x0814b612`/`0x0814b70e`, multiplies at `0x0814b631`, `0x0814b653`, `0x0814b723`, `0x0814b749`. The verifier confirmed the gate, the split, the `getTeam` call and the `+0x2cc` multiply, and additionally found an **early-out on `[this+0x2ec]` at `0x0814b535`** that the research pass did not report; it did not confirm that all four offsets map to (soldier/vehicle × splash/non-splash) as claimed |
| HP-9d | **`hasCollisionEffect` is the impact-versus-fuse discriminator, not a splash-capability flag.** Requiring it for splash generally would silently delete grenade, explosives-pack, satchel and landmine splash — the most-used splash damage in the game. The rule is two-path: impact explosion ⇔ `damageType == 1 && hasCollisionEffect`; end-of-life explosion ⇔ `damageType ∈ {1,4}`, with no `hasCollisionEffect` test, an untruncated radius and `sourceArmor = NULL` | **confirmed** (2026-09-19) | Gates `0x08153e79`/`0x08153ea9`; end-of-life `0x0831f6bb`/`0x0831f6c0`. Survey of every installed mod's `Objects.rfa`: **3,267** `damageType 1` templates across 16 mods, **3,016** of them with the flag, and **49** `damageType 4`. Vanilla has 28 `damageType 1`, 25 with the flag; the three without are exactly the fuse weapons — `ExpPackProjectile` (radius 12, material2 204), `GrenadeAlliesProjectile` and `GrenadeAxisProjectile` (15, 205) — plus `LandmineProjectile`, which is `damageType 4` (radius 4, material2 232). Six vanilla tank rounds (`ShermanProjectile`, `TigerProjectile`, `PanzerIVProjectile`, `T34Projectile`, `T34-85Projectile`, `Chi-ha_Projectile`) set no radius at all and ride the 10.0 constructor default, so that default is load-bearing |
| HP-9e | **`dieAfterColl` is read, and it is not a restatement of `hasCollisionEffect`.** `ProjectileTemplate+0x1a7`, bool, `ConsoleClass385`, instance `0x087a4fc0`, accessor `0x082de400`. `Projectile::handleCollision` recycles a round when `dieAfterColl` **or** `hasCollisionEffect` is set, through `resetProjectile`, which sets the detonate latch `Projectile+0x10d` and despawns **without** calling `startEndEffect` — so such a round explodes neither way, and a later `detonate()` returns on the latch. **Only a round with neither word survives contact to reach its fuse:** in vanilla exactly `GrenadeAlliesProjectile`, `GrenadeAxisProjectile`, `ExpPackProjectile` and `LandmineProjectile`. Named in the same chain and not consumed: `dieAtObjectHit` `+0x1a8` (`0x0831ef3c`), `isSticky` `+0x1ab` (`0x0831ef16`, attaches to the struck object and disables physics), `detonateOnWaterCollision` `+0x1ac` (`0x0831f3ae`; without it `handleCollision` returns immediately on water) — no vanilla projectile sets any of the three. Survey: 2,311 `dieAfterColl` declarations across 18 installs, values 0 and 1 only, **not** aligned with `hasCollisionEffect` — 1,676 templates carry `damageType 1` + `hasCollisionEffect 1` + `dieAfterColl 0`, including every vanilla bomb | **confirmed** (2026-09-20; the research pass filed it `open` with "what the engine does with it is UNVERIFIED", which the review then read) | `Projectile::handleCollision` `0x0831ee80`: tests at `0x0831ef4b` (`dieAfterColl`) and **`0x0831ef54`** (`hasCollisionEffect`), kill branch `0x0831ef5d` → `0x0831f006`, `resetProjectile` `0x0831e720` (latch written `0x0831e753`). `Projectile::detonate` `0x0831e680` is the only caller of `startEndEffect` and is reached from `handleUpdate` (ttl), `handleMessage`, `FireArms::detonateProjectiles`, `FireArms::placeScoutCamera` and `ProjectileNetworkable::setNetUpdate` — **none a collision path**. Ordering: `SimpleObject::handleCollision` runs first (`0x081ef01`), so the impact explosion and the direct hit still land before the recycle. Survey script `scratchpad/w2b-damage/survey_dieaftercoll.py` |
| HP-9f | **Vanilla has four `damageType 4` templates, not one, and the flag on the three flak shells is NOT dead.** `LandmineProjectile` (flag 0, `dieAfterColl 0`, radius 4, material2 232, ttl 360) plus `AA_Allies_Projectile` and `Carrier_AA_Projectile` (flag 1, `dieAfterColl 1`) and `Flak38_Projectile` (flag 1, `dieAfterColl` unset) — all three radius 20, material2 199, ttl `CRD_UNIFORM/0.8/1.4`. Type 4 gets no impact explosion, but the flag is consulted by HP-9e's recycle and **deletes the round on contact**: a flak shell that touches an aircraft, the ground or a wall takes its direct hit, plays its collision effect and vanishes. **The flag is what makes a timed airburst behave like one** — the first reading, "the flag is set on them and is dead", was wrong, and building on it burst flak shells where they landed | **confirmed and corrected** (2026-09-20) | Nothing explodes a type-4 round on contact: the gate at `0x08153e79` (`cmp [eax+0x160],1; je`) sends anything but 1 to the no-explosion tail at `0x08153e82`. Survey over vanilla `Objects.rfa` (`scratchpad/rw2b-damage/survey.py`): 28 `damageType 1`, 25 of them with the flag, and 4 `damageType 4`. corrects the research pass, which missed an 8.0 and a second formula) | `handleCollisionLandOrWater` `0x08154960`. **Soldier:** `severity = Q² · A · (Armor.speedMod · (\|v\|−8)²) · damageMod(att,def) · materialDamage(att)`, delivered only when `severity > 1.0`. **A soldier's impact speed has 8.0 subtracted first and the engine returns with no damage at all if that goes negative** — the CID test at `0x08154a81` jumps to `0x08155189`, which does `fsub ds:0x86c08c0` (`0x086c08c0` = `00 00 00 41` = **8.0f**), overwrites `\|v\|` at `0x0815519b` and returns at `0x0815519e` when the result is negative, so every downstream use (the kinetic term, the 30.0 saturation, the 10/20 lerp) uses the **reduced** value. `A = \|cosθ\|³` on land (`0x08154a93`–`0x08154ab2`) and **`\|cosθ\|²` in water** — `arg6 == 1` at `0x08154a89` branches to a duplicate of the whole path at `0x08154e4f` whose single `fmul st,st(0)` at `0x08154e60` squares instead of cubing; `A` is lerped to 1.0 over `2 ≤ F < 3` (`0x08154e16`) and saturated at `F ≥ 3` (`0x08154e3b`), then lerped again over `10 < \|v\| ≤ 30` (`0x08154dc6`–`0x08154dfb`) and saturated above 30 (`0x08154d9d`). **`Q²`** is `d8 ca` (`fmul st,st(2)`) then `de ca` (`fmulp st(2),st`) at `0x08154dbb`/`0x08154dbd` with `st(2) = Q`, x87 stack traced from `0x08154d20` by two agents independently. Threshold `fucom st(1)` against 1.0 at `0x08154c6b`, `je` taken only when `severity > 1.0`. `Armor::getSpeedMod` is vtable `+0x4c`, `0x08173fc0`, called at `0x08154c06`; `getDamageMod(arg6,arg7)` `0x08154bf8`; `getDamageForMaterial(arg6)` `0x08154c29`. **Non-soldier:** `severity = A · (speedMod·\|v\|²) · M1 · M2` (`0x08154c4f`), no height term and no 8.0 — the vehicle case, its object-vs-object twin and the material data are COL-3/COL-4 and [subsystems/collision-response.md](subsystems/collision-response.md) §9.3–9.5. | HP-15 | `SimpleObject+0xed` / `+0xee` are **persistent state, not per-frame edge flags**. `+0xed != 0` makes `PlayerControlObject::handlePlayerInput` return before forwarding input to any child — **a destroyed vehicle cannot be driven**. `+0xee != 0` makes `RotationalBundle::handlePlayerInput` scale all three input axes by **0.2** — **a critically damaged vehicle traverses at one fifth speed**. Both are cleared only when the wreck-respawn timer expires | **confirmed** (2026-09-19; supersedes ARM-6) | `PlayerControlObject::handlePlayerInput` `0x08318920`/`0x08318927` → epilogue `0x08318952`. `RotationalBundle::handlePlayerInput` `0x081d834f` selects between **two near-identical duplicated blocks** (`0x081d835c`, `0x081d8487`) that both decode the input and write `this+0x11c`; the `+0xee != 0` one additionally multiplies each of the three axes by the double at `ds:0x86c8678` = `9a9999999999c93f` = **0.2** (`0x081d83af`/`0x081d83b7`) — so `+0xee` means "traverse at 20% input", not "no input", and the research pass's "the block runs only when `+0xee` is non-zero" was a misreading. The clears at `0x081db3f1`/`0x081db3f8` sit behind **six** conditions inside `SimpleObject::handleUpdate` `0x081db2e0` — `+0xed != 0` (`0x081db33e`), `template+0x107 == 0`, `+0x100` already latched, a countdown `[this+0xfc] -= dt` falling below zero (`0x081db39f`–`0x081db3b1`), `template+0xd0 != 0`, and an Armor present — and are followed by `Armor::setHitPoints(Armor::getMaxHitPoints())` (vtable `+0x18`/`+0x14`) and a timer reload from `template+0xc4`: that is the **wreck-respawn timer**, not a per-frame clear. | ARM-1 | `addArmorEffect <hp> <effect> <x/y/z>` — the smoke/fire/death tiers — is stored per template, folded into a map on the Armor, and **re-evaluated every 30 Hz tick for as long as the object is alive** | **confirmed** (the per-tick cadence corrected 2026-09-17 after a first pass read the branch backwards; re-derived a third time by the lead from `objdump`) | `SimpleObjectTemplate::addArmorEffect` `0x081dded0` appends to three parallel per-template vectors (`+0x98` `vector<string>` name, `+0xa4` `vector<Vec3>` offset, `+0xb0` `vector<int>` threshold), confirmed by the mangled `_M_insert_aux` callees — separate from `setArmorComponent`'s 23 setters (HP-3). They fold into an `std::map<int,DamageEffects*>` at `Armor+0x148`, read by `Armor::getEffect(int)` `0x08172820` as a nearest-threshold-below lookup. `Armor::playEffect()` `0x08172960` compares the applicable tier against the last-shown field `Armor+0x50` and rebuilds the live effect instances; its one call site is inside `Armor::update()` at `0x08173100`. **The `+0x128` byte is a death latch, not a first-run latch.** `playEffect`'s own test is `flds 0x38(%edx)` / `flds 0x86c0308` (0.001) / `fxch` / `fucom %st(1)` / `test $0x45,%ah` / `je 8172e6d`: `test` clears ZF for every x87 result except *greater*, so the jump is taken exactly when `hp > 0.001`. That target (`0x8172e6d`–`0x8172ec7`) is the **alive** path — `fnstcw`, `or $0x800` for round-up, `frndint`, `fistpl`, then `jmp` into the same single `getEffect` call — and it never writes `+0x128`. The `movb $0x1,0x128(%edx)` at `0x81729a2` is on the fallthrough, the **death** path, beside the `push $0xffffffff` of `getEffect(-1)`. Every literal write to `+0x128` in `0x08172000`–`0x08178000`: `0x8172231`/`0x8172381` (the two constructors, 0), `0x81729a2` (1, death), `0x8173aed` (0, inside `status()` right after `push $0x14`) — so `status()` clears it on the critical-transition messages and a repaired vehicle resumes per-tick evaluation. `Armor+0x38` is current `hitPoints`. Identical on the client (`playEffect` `0x004bc3d0`, latch write `0x004bc419`, `update` `0x004bc650`, `status` `0x004bbad0`) |

| ARM-4 | The **client** `Armor` is a field-for-field port of the server's: same offsets (`+0x28`, `+0x38`, `+0x3c`, `+0x50`, `+0x128`, `+0x129`, `+0x148`), same 82-slot vtable, and no GCC 8-byte RTTI header — so a client dump-offset equals an lnxded call-offset directly. Its CID is `0xc4a5`, not lnxded's `0xc4a4` | **confirmed** | Factory `0x0049cc80` (`"dice.ref2.world.Armor"`), allocator `0x0049b280` (`operator new(464)`, matching lnxded's 464-byte pool), ctor `0x004bc240` (writes vtable `0x008dc7a8`, `+0x148` = empty-map sentinel `0x0097d6c8`). Methods: `getHitPoints` `0x005b7df0`, `setHitPoints` `0x004bbf80`, `damage` `0x004bbfe0`, `heal` `0x004bc040`, `status` `0x004bbad0`, `update` `0x004bc650`, `playEffect` `0x004bc3d0`, `getEffect` `0x004bc2f0`, `collision()` `0x004bba20`, `setLastCollisionHeight` `0x0072fc10`, `getLastCollisionHeight` `0x00836050`. `Armor::update` runs on the client's own 30 Hz sim tick (`GameClient::simulateFrame` `0x004b6cb0` → objectManager `+0x14` `updateObjects(tickDt,0,3)`), not per rendered frame |
| ARM-6 | ~~**Nothing in the drivetrain or the turret path consults a vehicle's Armor**, so a critically damaged vehicle drives and traverses exactly as a healthy one~~ — **RETIRED 2026-09-19 as false. Superseded by HP-15.** A critically damaged vehicle traverses at **0.2×** input and a destroyed one accepts **no player input at all**. The row is kept for its history: its sweep was sound and its conclusion still holds for the question it actually asked — no drivetrain or `RotationalBundle` function queries the Armor *component*. It missed the path because the Armor's state reaches the input code through `handleMessage` (HP-13's `0x14`/`0x15`) as two bytes on the object, `SimpleObject+0xed`/`+0xee`, which a `getComponent(0xc4a4)` sweep cannot see | **retired — see HP-15** (the original evidence, still true as a negative, follows) | Every `push $0xc4a4` (the `getComponent(0xc4a4,0xc4a4)` argument pair) in `bf1942_lnxded.static` mapped to its enclosing symbol: 145 sites across 56 functions on the lead's sweep, 86/52 on a narrower agent sweep, and neither contains a drivetrain or `RotationalBundle` function. The five physics subnodes that *do* query Armor — `Engine`, `Wing`, `Spring`, `Bundle`, `FloatingBundle` — do it only inside `handleMessage`, and the call after `getComponent` is vtable `+0x94` = `Armor::isSendingMessage()` (`0x081741c0`, resolved against the 82-slot dump; `Engine::handleMessage` `0x0823e730`, `Wing::handleMessage` `0x08250bf0`): message plumbing, not a damage gate |
| ARM-7 | Armor predicates the rest of the engine does read: `isDestroyed()` gates **entry-point validation**, and `PlayerControlObject::enter()` queries `isCriticalDamaged()` | **confirmed (`isDestroyed`, and the slot identities); inferred (`enter`'s consequence)** | Slots resolved against Armor's 82-slot vtable: call `+0xc8` → `isDestroyed()` `0x08174300`, `+0xcc` → `isCriticalDamaged()` `0x08174320`, `+0xd4` → `getHpLostWhileCriticalDamage()` `0x08174390`. `validateBFEntryPoint` (`0x0831d5f0`, call at `0x831d68f`) and `BFfindEntryPoint` (`0x0831d770`, call at `0x831d911`) each `getComponent` then test `+0xc8` and reject on true — you cannot spawn into or select a wrecked entry point. This closes `seats-and-entry-points.md`'s own Open item, which had the check's existence but not what it tested. `PlayerControlObject::enter` calls `+0xcc` at `0x8317095` on `[this+0x60]` and, on true, `+0xd4` at `0x83172b9` before rejoining the normal flow — what consumes that float is untraced, and `[this+0x60]`'s type rests on the slot match, so the *consequence* of entering a burning vehicle is open. `exit(bool)` also queries Armor (`0x83180b7`) but calls `setLastCollisionHeight` (`+0xf8`), unrelated |

### Friendly fire (2026-09-24)

Whether a round meets and hurts a soldier of its firer's own side, what scales that damage, and whether a bot holds its fire for a teammate. Asked after XHIT-4 made the local player's rounds meet his teammates while the viewer's bots still skipped their own side (`vehicle-hits.js` `roundBodyCast`, `bot-referee.js` `resolveShot`; the skip came in with cc10a91e as a convenience). All addresses `bf1942_lnxded.static` (sha256 `49667237…c447cf2`), which is where damage is decided (HP-6). What was built: [features/bot-friendly-fire/README.md](../bot-friendly-fire/README.md).

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| FF-1 | **A round meets any soldier in its path, friend or foe. Nothing on the way from contact to damage compares teams.** The candidates are every object near the round's sweep, less the round itself, its own root, anything sharing a collision group with it (FF-2) and anything with object flag bit 0. The contact test skips exactly one kind of soldier: one whose root is the root of the firer's controlled object. That is his own body on foot, and anyone seated in the hull he fires from, because a seated soldier is a child of his seat. Dead soldiers are skipped. The damage path has no team test either: nearest Armor, nothing for a destroyed one, and direct damage given only above 0.01 | **confirmed** | `ResponsePhysicsManager::checkObjectVsObjects` `0x0825d820`: `getCollisionObjects`, skip self and `getRootParent(candidate) == round`, skip when `(round groups & candidate groups) != 0` or `[candidate+4] & 1`, then `checkObjectVsObject` (vt+0x60). `PointResponsePhysics::checkObjectVsObject` `0x08257030`: for a `CID_BFSoldierTemplate` (0x9493) candidate and a `CID_ProjectileTemplate` (0x9495) round, `playerManager->getPlayer(round+0x108)`, `getRootParent(player->getVehicle())` (IPlayer vt+0x3c = `BFPlayer::getVehicle` `0x080560c0`) against `getRootParent(soldier)`, `cmp ebx,eax; jne` at `0x08257708` (equal: no contact). The soldier is tested at `BFSoldier::getLatencyPosition` against col LOD 1 (`response vt+0x58(1)`) and only `isSolidMaterial` faces count. Seated soldier as a child: `SeatObject::enter` `0x083208d0` takes `player->getVehicle()` (his soldier), adds it as a child of the seat (vt+0x80) and gives it an identity transform. `Projectile::handleCollision` `0x0831ee80` passes a soldier whose `+0x245` byte is set, and `BFSoldier::handleDamage` `0x08270980` sets that byte on the lethal hit (`0x08270bcb`) and returns at once while it is set. `GameServer::handleCollisionForProjectile` `0x08153ba0` direct branch: nearest Armor by `getComponent(0xc4a4)`, `isDestroyed` (Armor vt+0xc8) true gives effect only; `cmp edi,eax; je` at `0x081540cf` (the round's root equal to the struck root: nothing); damage `(angleMod + (1 − angleMod)·sin(abs(cos)·π/2)) · getDamageMod · Projectile::getDamage`; `fucompp` against 0.01f (`0x086c08a4`) at `0x0815427b`, taken only when greater; then `giveDamage(Armor::getObject(), dmg, round+0x108, round+0x12c, round+0x140, pos, material, 1, canTK)` at `0x081544bc`. The round's owner, team and stat id are stamped by `FireArms::fireBarrel` at `0x0828b4e3` (`+0x108`), `0x0828b4ec` (`+0x12c` = the firing `BFPlayer+0x7c`, his team) and `0x0828b582` (`+0x140`) |
| FF-2 | **Collision groups are object classes, never teams.** `ObjectTemplate.addToCollisionGroup N` ORs N into the template's `+0x78`, and every object's response physics gets its template's mask when it is built. The constants are `c_CGLandscape` 1, `c_CGStaticObjects` 2, `c_CGLadders` 4, `c_CGProjectiles` 8. Code adds two more: every projectile template is in `c_CGProjectiles`, and a climbing soldier joins `c_CGLadders`. So a round (8) against any soldier (0, or 4 on a ladder) always passes the mask test, and statics marked `c_CGProjectiles` are what rounds fly through | **confirmed** | `ConsoleClass86::executeObjectMethod` `0x081bd440` (`or [tmpl+0x78], arg`). `SimpleObjectTemplate::setResponsePhysicsComponent` `0x081dd1d0`: all three response kinds (`ResponsePhysics`, `StaticResponsePhysics`, `PointResponsePhysics` at `0x081dd364`) rejoin at `0x081dd21c`, which calls `addToCollisionGroup(tmpl+0x78)` (IResponsePhysics vt+0x30) at `0x081dd24d`; `PointResponsePhysics` keeps the mask at `+0x14` (ctor zeroes it). `addConstantHelper` values at `0x08299447`/`0x08299476`/`0x082994a5`/`0x082994d0`. `ProjectileTemplate::ProjectileTemplate` `or esi,0x8` into `+0x78` at `0x0831faad`; `BFSoldier::startClimbing` `0x08281b20` and `handleClimbAction` `0x08281080` push 4 before a vt+0x30 call. Survey of every `addToCollisionGroup` in vanilla, XPack1 and XPack2 (`scratchpad` `survey_groups.py`, 51 / 6 / 16 uses): all on static `SimpleObject`/`Obstacle` templates (fences, ladders, palms, barbed wire, camouflage nets), none on a soldier, vehicle or projectile. The other 15 installs use the same four constants |
| FF-3 | **Friendly damage is the damage times a ratio, and only a same-team hit is scaled.** `GameServer::calcDamage(obj, dmg, team, splash)` scales only when the round's team is 1 or 2 and equals `getTeam()` of the `PlayerControlObject` at the victim's root. A soldier root takes the soldier ratio. Anything else takes the vehicle ratio, including a man seated in a hull, whose root is the hull. Splash takes the `OnSplash` pair. Each ratio is its console percentage divided by 100 and clamped to [0, 2]. **The shipped `Mods/bf1942/Settings/ServerSettings.con` sets all four to 100, so a friendly round costs exactly what an enemy one does.** The four offsets of HP-9c are re-derived here | **confirmed** | `calcDamage` `0x0814b520`: `dec eax; cmp eax,1; ja` (return untouched) at `0x0814b545`; root template `getClassID` against `CID_BFSoldierTemplate` (`ds:0x86c2b88` = 0x9493) at `0x0814b604`; `getTeam` (PCO vt+0x74) `cmp eax,[ebp+0x14]` at `0x0814b618`/`0x0814b648`; `fmul [+0x2cc]` at `0x0814b631` (vehicle), `fmul [+0x2c8]` at `0x0814b653` (soldier); splash `+0x2d0`/`+0x2d4`. Setters `setSoldierFFRatio` `0x081572d0` (+0x2c8), `setVehicleFFRatio` `0x08157310` (+0x2cc), `setSoldierFFRatioOnSplash` `0x08157360` (+0x2d0), `setVehicleFFRatioOnSplash` `0x081573b0` (+0x2d4), each clamping to [0, 2]. Wiring: `game.serverSoldierFriendlyFire` → `Setup+0x29c` (`ConsoleClass531` `0x08084a30`), `serverVehicleFriendlyFire` → `+0x2a0` (`ConsoleClass532` `0x08084e30`), `serverSoldierFriendlyFireOnSplash` → `+0x2e8` (`ConsoleClass554` `0x0808a2d0`), `serverVehicleFriendlyFireOnSplash` → `+0x2ec` (`ConsoleClass555` `0x0808a6d0`); `Setup::initWorld` `0x080be4c0` `fild`s the first two and divides by 100.0 (`ds:0x86b01ac`) into `startHostGame`'s 3rd/4th floats, which reach IGameServer slots +0x130/+0x138; `Setup::startHostGame` `0x080c4080` divides the splash pair by 100 into slots +0x140/+0x148 (secondary vptr at GameServer vtable symbol + 0x494; the thunks sit at +0x5c4/+0x5cc/+0x5d4/+0x5dc). `giveDamage` `0x0814b2e0` calls `calcDamage(obj, dmg, team, false)` before `_giveDamage`; explosions call it with the splash flag (HP-9, HP-9c). The `+0x2ec` early-out is `GameServer::setGodMode` `0x0813dc10`: every object of the local player takes 0, off unless set |
| FF-4 | **Friendly damage is not reflected on the shooter.** `_giveDamage` damages the attacker by `damage × serverKickBack` (splash: `× serverKickBackOnSplash`) when the victim's player is a different player of the attacker's team, the caller allows it (a round's direct hit does) and the product exceeds 0.01. The shipped file sets both to 0.000000 | **confirmed** | `_giveDamage` `0x0814b870` (bools: `param_11` splash, which `giveDamage` passes as 0; `param_12` allow, which `handleCollisionForProjectile` passes as 1; `param_13` canTK): same-team test `BFPlayer+0x7c` equality over the victim PCO's players, `fmul [+0x2d8]` (`setKickBack` `0x08157400`) / `fmul [+0x2dc]` (`setKickBackOnSplash` `0x08157420`), `<= 0.01` skips, then `giveDamage(attacker's object, kick, -1, …)`. The pair reaches GameServer through IGameServer slots +0x150/+0x158 from `Setup+0x2e0`/`+0x2e4`, undivided. Shipped `ServerSettings.con`: `game.serverKickBack 0.000000`, `game.serverKickBackOnSplash 0.000000` |
| FF-5 | **Every friendly kill by a round counts as a team kill.** `Projectile::canTK` is true while the round is younger than its template's `timeToRemoveTK`, or always when that is −1, and it rides to the victim's Armor as `setLastHitCanTK`. The constructor default is −1.0 and no installed mod sets the word | **confirmed** | `canTK` `0x0831f870` (`+0x1c8` against `WorldPref::mWorldTime − round+0x124`); `ObjectTemplate.timeToRemoveTK` writes `ProjectileTemplate+0x1c8` (`ConsoleClass410::executeObjectMethod` `0x082e3dd0`); ctor `mov [ebx+0x1c8],0xbf800000` at `0x0831fa5d`. `_giveDamage` calls Armor vt+0x74 `setLastHitPlayer`, vt+0x84 `setLastHitDamageType` and vt+0x7c `setLastHitCanTK` (`0x081740e0`, `Armor+0x30`). Survey of all 18 installs: zero `timeToRemoveTK` |
| FF-6 | A soldier struck by a round of his own team plays sound-trigger entry 0x13 of his `+0xf8` set, at most once every 3 s. Entry 0x13 is `c_SstHitDamage` in the server's table, and `BFSoldier::handleDamage` also plays 0x13 through `+0x4f8`, so the two may be one sound. Vanilla ships `SoldierFFHitDamage.ssc` (loaded by `SoldierSound.inc`), and nothing here selects it. How the client picks the friendly-fire grunt is not traced | **confirmed (the call and its gate); open (which sample the client plays)** | `Projectile::handleCollision` calls `BFSoldier::projectileHit(round+0x12c)` `0x0827ea20` for a soldier when the projectile template's `+0x14f` byte is clear. `projectileHit` requires the soldier's player `+0x3e8`, `getBFPlayer(...)+0x7c == team` and `worldTime − [+0x56c] > 3.0`. `c_SstHitDamage` registered as 0x13 at `0x08298fed`; no `c_SstFFHitDamage` string in the server or in `BF1942.exe` |
| FF-7 | **The bot AI has no friendly-fire avoidance: a bot fires through a teammate in its line of fire.** Targets are enemies only (`SideFilter`, AI-33). The fire plans gate the trigger on distance, a valid aim, `ObjectLineOfFire` and the precision conditions, and none of these tests a team or casts a ray. `ObjectLineOfFire` is the memory record's seen flag (AI-61, AI-116). The one ray-based line of fire, `BAPConLineOfFire`, is built only by the TakeCover plan. Its ray (`collideLineWithWorld`) ignores the bot's own objects and, with its flag set, moving ones, and it has no team test either. The server's settings (FF-3) alone decide what such a round costs | **confirmed** | Condition classes (every `BAPCon*`/`BAPCCon*::evaluate`) checked for team, side and friend calls: `BAPCConPrecision` `0x0854b570`, `BAPCConPrecision3d` `0x0854baf0`, `BAPCConLookDeviation` `0x0854b070`, `BAPConWeaponReadyToFire` `0x08555a80`, `BAPConWeaponTargetReachable` `0x08555d60`, `BAPConObjectVisible` `0x08553a40`, `BAPConObjectLineOfFire` `0x08551890`: none. `BAPConGeneric1arg`/`2arg` (callback conditions) are never constructed. The trigger entries `EntryTrigger::execute` `0x086257d0` and `EntryTriggerContinously::execute` `0x08625ff0` drive the input word from the plan's start and stop conditions only. `BAPConObjectLineOfFire` ctors are called from `BBPFire3d::createPlanInternal` (`0x0859d7df`, `0x0859d8b6`), `BBPFireInfantery::createPlanInternal` (`0x085a98b9`, `0x085a9cbc`) and TakeCover. `BAPConLineOfFire` ctors only from `BBPTakeCoverInfantry::createPlan` (`0x085cb3a6`, `0x085cba23`, `0x085cbabf`). `BAPConLineOfFire::evaluate` `0x0854ec50` → `BFEnvironment::collideLineWithWorld` `0x085e3320` (env vt+0x54, radius 0.75, flag 1): terrain first, then `objectManager` vt+0x50/+0x4c with `RayCastStopPredicate` `0x085e6a00` / `SphereExcludeFilter` `0x085e6940`, which exclude the ignore list and, when flagged, objects whose plug-in `+0x14` exceeds 0.1. `objFireLineDist` `0x084637c0` is the near-miss sense (`handleIndirectProjectileHit` `0x08464500`), not a fire gate |

No team test in the function |

## Rigid-body collisions (2026-09-19; the client's `handleCollision` override stays unlocated)

How two bodies collide: contact finding, the mass-ratio push, integration,
sleeping, friction and crash damage. Narrative:
[subsystems/collision-response.md](subsystems/collision-response.md); reports
and verdicts: [`features/vehicle-collision-physics/`](../vehicle-collision-physics/README.md).
All addresses `bf1942_lnxded.static` unless marked client. "Verified" here means
re-derived from `objdump` by a second agent; COL-8 was additionally run as
machine code under an emulator.

| # | Finding, or the assumption it replaces | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| COL-2 | The viewer treats another vehicle as a fixed obstacle (`viewer/ground.js` hull `sweepSphere`). The engine splits the correction by mass: `shareA = massB/(massA+massB)` from the two ROOT nodes, `shareB = -(1 - shareA)`, snapped to (1, 0) above 0.95 and to (0, **+1.0**) below 0.05 — the positive sign is an engine slip — then both times a direction weight (COL-6) | **confirmed** (V0, V3) | `ResponsePhysics::checkObjectVsObject` `0x08259690`: `0x08259908`–`0x082599a4` (raw `de f1`, `d8 ea`, `xorb $0x80`), low snap `0x0825a3be`–`0x0825a3e5` (`fldz; fxch; fstps`); constants `0x086d16cc` 0.95, `0x086c08a8` 0.05. A static node reports mass 1e12, so a static always snaps to (1, 0). Client twin `0x00574e30`, same constants |
| COL-3 | `vehicle-damage.js`: "do not add a crash-damage path — the engine has none". Object vs object: `damage = attackerArmor.damageMod x (angleMod + (1 - angleMod) sin(abs(cos) pi/2)) x victim.speedMod x v^2 x getDamageMod(matAttacker, matVictim) x getDamageForMaterial(matAttacker)`, applied when `> 1.0`; the *other* object is the attacker; mass does not appear | **confirmed** (V0; numbers V4) | `GameServer::handleCollisionObjectVsObject` `0x081551c0`: product `0x08155752`–`0x0815576a`, gate `0x0815585a`–`0x08155867`, `giveDamage` `0x081558d6`; argument swap in `SimpleObject::handleCollision` `0x081dab40`. Soldier victim: `V' = attackerProj + max(victimProj - 8, 0)`, no attacker `damageMod`, a fall-height and kit-damping term squared. Willy at 15 m/s into a parked Spitfire: 45 HP to the plane, 22.5 to the jeep |
| COL-4 | `viewer-collision-damage` behaviour 1, "a plane that hits the ground ... not in the engine". Terrain: `cos^3 x speedMod x v^2 x getDamageMod(matTerrain, matSelf) x getDamageForMaterial(matTerrain)`, `> 1.0`; water `cos^2`, needs `damageFromWater`, no gate; a soldier subtracts 8 m/s first. Vehicles are not excluded | **confirmed** (V0; numbers V4) | `GameServer::handleCollisionLandOrWater` `0x08154960`. Terrain `materialDamage` is 30 for all 16 terrain materials; cells terrain -> 45/50/60 are 0.01, -> 61/63 0.1. Spitfire at 40 m/s, 30 deg below the horizon: 120 to 1,200 against 100 HP |
| COL-5 | Collision damage is limited to **one event per (Armor, other object) per second**; terrain is the entry `NULL` | **confirmed** (V0) | `Armor::addColObject` `0x08173740` / `isInColList` `0x081744d0`: 16-slot ring, objects `+0x54`, delta timers `+0x94`, head `+0xd4`, tail `+0xd8`, expiry an `fld1` literal; popped in `Armor::update` `0x08172f40`. `handleCollision` skips the dispatch when the other object is listed |
| COL-6 | Contacts are the vertex side's **col0 vertices** probed against the face side's **col1** faces (col0 when the vertex side's root bounding radius >= 4.0 and it is not a soldier). The smaller body (root radius ratio > 4) or the soldier is the vertex side, weight 1.0; similar sizes are tested **both ways at 0.5 each**; a static is always the face side. The probe is a ray from `rootA.pos - relV dt` to the vertex's current position, single-sided, depth = the end point's plane distance | **confirmed** (V3) | `ResponsePhysicsManager::checkObjectVsObjects` `0x0825d820` (ten pair filters, direction tree `0x0825dcc8`–`0x0825de21`), `checkObjectVsObject` `0x08259690`, `SimpleCollisionMesh::getDistanceToGeometry` `0x083c9f70`, `getVertexCollision` / `getFaceCollision` `0x082587e0` / `0x08258730`. The selected LOD is stored on the shared mesh template (`+0xe4`) |
| COL-7 | "col0 projectile / col1 vehicle / col2 soldier" (community tools, and comments in our readers). **No `.sm` has a third collision layer and the server never asks for one** | **refuted** (V3: 0 of 46,858 `.sm` entries across 18 mods) | `.sm` collision index i is engine collision LOD i (`loadCollision` `0x083a6290`); a whole-binary scan of calls through response vtable `+0x58`/`+0x5c` finds only the immediates 0 and 1. A collision vertex is xyz + **u16 material** at `+0xc`, which `stdmesh.py` reads as a float and discards; both sides of a contact bring their own material (vertex / face) |
| COL-8 | Only the **root** `PhysicsNode` integrates, one semi-implicit Euler step per tick; rotation is `dw = sum_axis proj(T, axis) dt / (inertiaModifier.axis x I.axis)` with `I` from the mesh bounding box (`(DY^2+DZ^2)/3` ...) — **mass never enters rotation**; the body turns about its origin; `w` stays in world axes | **confirmed** (emulated: lnxded's own code under Unicorn vs a Python model, worst error 6.5e-6; re-run by V2) | `PhysicsNode::updatePhysics` `0x082543d0`, `updatePositionalPhysics` `0x08253570`, `updateRotationalPhysics` `0x082539e0`, `getGeometryInertia` `0x08253930`; clamps 1e6 -> 1000 (acc, torque), friction zeroed above 62500 / 40000. Harness: `features/vehicle-collision-physics/tools/emulator/` |
| COL-9 | The response: `impulseOn` cancels the closing speed along the normal and pushes out by the penetration, merged per axis by `setAdjust` (same sign: larger magnitude; opposite: add); `solveImpulse` moves the position now and posts `speedAdjust x 30 x (1 + elasticity)/2` as an acceleration **at the contact point** on the ROOT node, integrated next tick. Elasticity is 0 for every vehicle and terrain material | **confirmed** (V0) | `impulseOn` `0x08258900`, `setAdjust` `0x0825cec0`, `solveImpulse` `0x08258d30` (gated on a non-zero positional adjust; wheels take the push-out along the normal only), `addAccelerationAtAbsolutePosition` `0x08255110`. Clients `0x00574bb0`, `0x005745b0`, `0x005765d0`, `0x00540220` |
| COL-10 | A parked vehicle is asleep (100 quiet ticks; awake while `acc^2 >= 2.5`, `v^2 >= 0.25` or `w^2 >= 0.25`). The resolve pass has no sleeping test and `addFriction` wakes the root when the contact speed^2 > 0.1, so a rammed sleeping vehicle moves **two ticks** after the touch; slower contacts only push it out | **confirmed** (V2, the wake sequence emulated) | `PhysicsNode::mFramesBeforeSafeToSleep` `0x0872deec` = 100; `ResponsePhysicsManager::update` `0x0825d0b0` pass 2 (`0x0825d581`); `addFriction` `0x0825bd1b`–`0x0825bdd1` |
| COL-11 | Tick order: controls, then engines/wings/springs accumulate on the root, then the root integrates, then contacts are detected and resolved; an occupied vehicle (`hasSeparatePhysicsUpdate`, set by `enterVehicle`) takes the per-player passes, everything else the global ones | **confirmed** (V2). **Refuted** within it: "a vehicle whose player sent no input is skipped that tick" — it is simulated with zero input | `GameServer::simulateFrame` `0x0815c2a0`; `Game::updateWorldCollision` `0x0805da00`; `performMobilePhysicsUpdate` `0x08147470`, `performMobileCollisionUpdate` `0x08147580`; `disablePhysics` / `reenablePhysics` `0x08147730` / `0x08147680`. `responsePhysicsManager` vtable: `update` is `+0x14`; the two `+0x1c` calls the corpus had recorded in `simulateFrame` unnamed are `resetCachedCollisionObjects` |
| COL-12 | The client runs the same contact and response code for every vehicle, local or remote, and does not compute collision damage | **confirmed** for the response (vtables match slot for slot, constants identical; slots re-read from the live binary by the lead); **inferred** for "no damage" (no code caller of `Armor::damage` / `getSpeedMod` / `addColObject`; the client's `handleCollision` override was not located) | client vtables `0x008fdce4` (manager), `0x008fd770` (response), `0x008f7218` (node); per-player pass `0x004b7870` |

## Vehicle seats and entry points (2026-09-16; the client-side dispatch address stays open; SEAT-9 extended and SEAT-25 added 2026-09-19; the team rule SEAT-26..SEAT-29 read and built 2026-09-25, correcting SEAT-4)

> **SEAT-14…SEAT-24 do not exist.** SEAT-25 keeps the number the occupant
> stream gave it, so that its report, its review and the seat-pose work all
> refer to the same row. Number the next seat finding SEAT-30.

Entering, switching seats in, and leaving anything a soldier can spawn into —
Defgun, Sherman, a ship's AA mount. Settled for the map viewer's `seats.js`.
Full narrative: [subsystems/seats-and-entry-points.md](subsystems/seats-and-entry-points.md).
All addresses `bf1942_lnxded.static` unless marked client.

| # | Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| SEAT-1 | Vehicle entry/exit is triggered by **`c_PIUse`** (`PlayerInputMap` index 10), not a distinctly-named "enter/exit" input; rising edge above the digital threshold 0.5 | **confirmed** | 56-slot `operator<<` jump table `0x081d89f0` and its string pool, read byte-for-byte; threshold `0x86b05e8`. `GameServer::checkPlayerTriggers` (`0x0814f2c0`) tests bit 10 first and it is the only trigger reaching `toggleEntryPoint` |
| SEAT-2 | A hard-coded **1.0-second per-player cooldown** gates `toggleEntryPoint`; the actual toggle call is **further gated** by a vehicle-state consistency check | **confirmed (both layers)** | Cooldown field `+0x98` against `WorldPref::mWorldTime` (`0x087435a0`). `toggleEntryPoint` (`0x0814ee70`) compares the player's cached vehicle (`+0x74`) against `player->getVehicle()` (vtable+0x3c); on a match it additionally requires a `GameServer+0x66` byte before calling `toggleEntryPoint(player, false)` at all — a naive "always toggle on press" diverges from retail around vehicle-transition edges |
| SEAT-3 | `findEntryPoint` picks the **nearest** in-range `EntryPoint`, excluding the player's own current vehicle's entry; ancestry walks climb an `ICompositeObject` parent chain looking for the nearest `IPlayerControlObject` | **confirmed** | `0x0818df50`; `IID_IEntryPointObject` `0x086c2a24` identifies both the exclusion target and filters candidates; parent chain at `+0x50`, `IID_ICompositeObject` `0x086c2a58`, `IID_IPlayerControlObject` `0x086d3c50` |
| SEAT-4 | `validateBFEntryPoint` chooses caller-radius vs. the `EntryPointTemplate`'s own `entryRadius` via a bool, range-tests against absolute position — **and separately gates on a team/hostility component check**, independent of `BFfindEntryPoint`'s own team/neutral test | **confirmed + new gate found; the gate CORRECTED 2026-09-25 (SEAT-28): it is `Armor::isDestroyed`, a wreck test, and `validateBFEntryPoint` has no team test** | `0x0831d590`; radius from template `+0x138`. Separate gate: `queryComponent(0xc4a4,0xc4a4)` (walked up the `+0x50` parent chain if needed), then `call [component_vtable+0xc8]`, reject on failure — a viewer needs **both** gates to reproduce retail's entry-point filtering |
| SEAT-5 | `isAllowedToExit()` is a **pure geometry/collision check** — no speed or altitude anywhere in it; the check runs only when the vehicle template's `hasRestrictedExit` is set, otherwise it returns true unconditionally | **confirmed** | `0x08317490`, 561 instructions read in full: `setRotation`×2, `Mat4::mult`×2, `getRootParent`×2 (submarine tail), geometry/hierarchy vtable accessors only. `hasRestrictedExit` at template `+0x192`. `getSubPos()` (vtable `+0x140`) is the genuine first check inside `isAllowedToExit`, but the equivalent call inside `exitPlayer()` (`0x083183d0`) is reached only deep inside one specific sub-path (remote occupant, no child, null root), not universally first there |
| SEAT-6 | `EntryPoint::enter`/`exit` are near-vestigial: `enter` ignores both parameters and forwards to the linked PCO's `init()`; `exit` unconditionally returns 1. `PlayerControlObject::enter(player, force)` refuses only when already occupied **and** `force==false` | **confirmed** | `0x081ae030`/`0x081ae070`; `0x08316f00` |
| SEAT-7 | Seat switching uses **`c_PIMenuSelect1..9`** (indices 14–22): target position id = key−1, looked up in a `map<int, IPlayerControlObject*>` — built by `init()`'s **six** helpers, conditionally, only for **root** PCOs | **confirmed** | `PlayerControlObject::init()` `0x08316790` calls `collectWeapons`, `findPlayerInputObjects` (unconditional), then — only when `this+0x50==0` — `postInitPcoId`, `postInitCameras`, `findPlayerControlObjects` (`0x08280880`, builds the map), `findSpawnPoints`. A non-root child PCO never rebuilds its own copy of the map. Also requires the target's own `init()`, a `GameServer+0x64` byte, and that the requester is not an AI player (vtable+0x5c) |
| SEAT-8 | `SeatFlags`: `HalfBody=0x1, FullBody=0x2, HeadOfSoldier=0x4, IsOutside=0x8, Standing=0x10`, stored at `SeatObjectTemplate+0x158` (`\|=` on set) | **confirmed** | `operator<<` `0x083207f0`; `setSeatFlags` `0x08321900`. `SeatObject::enter` passes `seatFlags=0` when none of `Half\|Full\|Head\|Standing` (mask `0x17`) are set |
| SEAT-9 | `+0x15c`=**upperBody**, `+0x160`=**lowerBody** animation-name strings feed `BFSoldier::setUseSeat`; empty strings fall back to numeric ids on the soldier's own template | **confirmed, resolved** | `setUseSeat` `0x08271950`; resolved via `SeatObject::enter`'s push order cross-checked against `setUseSeat`'s own branch semantics. Fallbacks: `+0x294` (lower/non-standing), `+0x298` (upper, always used), `+0x29c` (lower/standing). **Extended 2026-09-19: the three fallbacks are named states, and none of them is `Lb_Stand`** — `+0x294` is `Lb_SitInVehicle`, `+0x298` is `Ub_SitInVehicle`, `+0x29c` is `Lb_StandInVehicle`, each resolved by name in `BFSoldierTemplate::init` at `0x0827acaf`/`0x0827acff`/`0x0827ad4f` and spent by `setUseSeat` at `0x8271a02`/`0x8271a40`/`0x8271b43`. A viewer that falls back to the standing idle poses a passenger wrongly. `setUseSeat` also **skips the lower-body state entirely for a half-body seat** (`0x8271a1d` → `0x8271b63`), where it instead calls `disableBoneTree(getBoneNameIndex("Bip01 Pelvis"))` — resolving one anyway is harmless, since the legs are hidden |
| SEAT-10 | A seat's first camera reporting type `3` forces `setFirstPerson(true)`; **any other value explicitly forces it off** | **confirmed (both directions)** | `setUseSeat` compares a camera-list value to literal `3`; both branches explicitly call `setFirstPerson` — do not just leave the player's own toggle alone in the non-3 case |
| SEAT-11 | `enter(player, force=true)` steals an occupied seat, and **the only path that passes `true` is `GameServer::exitVehicle`**, re-entering the player's own soldier. The **seat switch passes `false`** — a `c_PIMenuSelect` to an occupied seat is refused, not stolen. So do `c_PIUse` entry, spawn and kill. The viewer's seat switch may keep refusing an occupied seat: that is retail | **confirmed** (2026-09-19) | `PlayerControlObject::enter` `0x08316f00`, reachable only through `IPlayerControlObject` vtable **vptr+0x20** (thunk `0x0831ab50` at `vtable for PlayerControlObject` `0x0873eec0`+0x1d8; the secondary vptr is sym+0x1b8, offset-to-top `0xfffffefc`, verified by resolving every slot in the group). Single call site `0x0814e90a` inside `GameServer::enterVehicle(BFPlayer*, IPlayerObject*, bool, bool)` `0x0814e860`, which forwards its **4th** parameter (`[ebp+0x18]` → `[ebp-0x16a]` at `0x0814e87b`; the 3rd goes to `[ebp-0x169]` and is not the force). `enterVehicle` is at `GameServer` vtable vptr+0x6c (read from `vtable for dice::bf::GameServer` `0x0871b0e0`). Callers with push order decoded (cdecl, first push = last argument): seat switch `checkPlayerTriggers` `0x0814fbb8` `(…,false,false)`; `toggleEntryPoint` `0x0814f193` `(…,false,false)`; `spawnPlayer` `0x0814cdcc` `(…,true,false)`; `killPlayer` `0x0814db4c` `(…,true,false)`; `exitVehicle` `0x0814e67b` `(…,false,true)`. No direct call to `enter` or to its thunk exists anywhere in the disassembly; that `0x0814e90a` is the *only* indirect `call [reg+0x20]` reaching it was not exhaustively proved, and no counter-example surfaced |
| SEAT-12 | Exit places the soldier at a per-seat `soldierExitLocation` offset+rotation. Every negative `exitTimer` across a full 16-archive survey (180 rows) is a boat/raft or an AI-only `Remote*` position | **confirmed (pattern)** | Boats/rafts: Daihatsu, Lcvp, Elco80Raft, Type38Raft, Barkasse, Riverboat, Sampan, CDNRaft, Sturmboot and others. Pattern holds without exception; `exitTimer`'s consuming function is unfound |
| SEAT-13 | `seatAnimationUpperBody`/`seatAnimationLowerBody` name the animation states `setUseSeat` resolves for a passenger seat; only passenger seats declare them (34 uses across 14 mods, 0 on a driver seat or manned gun) | **extracted** | Parsed by `con.py`, emitted as `extras.seat.poseAnimation`, captured as `seat.poseAnimation` in `viewer/seats.js` `surveyVehicle` |
| SEAT-25 | **A seat draws an occupant iff its PCO subtree reaches a `SeatObject`** — it is data, not naming — and `seatFlags` says how much of him: half 1, full 2, head 4, outside 8, standing 16. `Sherman`'s root declares EntryPoints, a Camera and the turret and **no SeatObject**, which is the whole of "the driver is invisible"; `shermanBrowning_PCO1` reaches `ShermanBrowningSeat` (`c_SeatShowHalfBodySoldier`), and `setUseSeat` `disableBoneTree`s `"Bip01 Pelvis"` for that flag. **Of the fifteen distinct `seatFlags` spellings across 16 mods, only five occur as strings anywhere in the binary** — `c_SeatShowCrouchingSoldier` (11 uses), `c_SeatHalfBodySoldier` (6, two of them vanilla: `Elco80SideGunnerSeat`, `Type38SideGunnerSeat`), `c_SeatShowSittingSoldier`, `c_SeatForceSittingSoldier`, `c_ShowHalfBodySoldier` and the case variants are unknown to the engine and declare nothing | **confirmed (the flags and the rule); the draw predicate's caller UNDETERMINED** | Jump table of `operator<<(ostream&, ISeatObjectTemplate::SeatFlags)` `0x083207f0`, table `0x086e1e3c` (`cmp eax,0x10`), all five entries read. `disableBoneTree(getBoneNameIndex("Bip01 Pelvis"))` at `0x8271b63` inside `setUseSeat` `0x08271950`, re-derived in full. **`hasSeatObject(IPlayerControlObject*)` exists at `0x0831dc00` and does exactly what the rule says — but it has no caller in lnxded**, which is what you would expect of a drawing concern on a dedicated server. Confirmed as a predicate the engine carries and consistent with every vanilla vehicle; the call site needs the client |
| SEAT-26 | **The entry rule: a player may take a seat only of a hull whose team is 0 or his own.** The team is the vehicle's ROOT `PlayerControlObject`'s (`getTeam`, IPlayerControlObject vt+0x74), the player's is `BFPlayer+0x7c`. `GameServer::toggleEntryPoint` tests it after whichever finder ran and returns without entering on a mismatch with a non-zero team (no cooldown stamped); `BFfindEntryPoint` already skips such a hull's entry points, so the nearest door that may be taken wins. `validateBFEntryPoint` (the bot's finder) has no team test; `GameServer::enterVehicle` compares nothing, so the seat switch (`checkPlayerTriggers`, inside the player's own hull) takes no rule | **confirmed** (2026-09-25) | `toggleEntryPoint` `0x0814ee70`: root found by `getRootParent` then `queryInterface(0xc4c5)` up the parents (`0x0814f100`..`0x0814f139`), `mov 0x7c(%esi),%ebx` `0x0814f142`, `call *0x74` `0x0814f146`, `cmp %eax,%ebx; je` `0x0814f14c`, `call *0x74; test; jne return` `0x0814f156`..`0x0814f15e`, then `enterVehicle(player, obj, false, false)` `0x0814f193` and the cooldown `0x0814f1a1`. `BFfindEntryPoint` `0x0831d770`: `call *0x74` `0x0831da58`, `cmp %eax,0x10(%ebp)` (the `int team` argument) `0x0831da5e`, `call *0x74; test; jne next` `0x0831da69`..`0x0831da71`; the human path passes radius 3.0, the player's team and `useTemplateRadius` true, a bot with `GameServer+0x64` set takes `validateBFEntryPoint(botTarget, pos, 15.0, false)` or `BFfindEntryPoint(pos, 15.0, team, false, 0, false)`. IPCO vtable (`PlayerControlObject` secondary vptr `0x0873f078`): +0x14 `isOccupied`, +0x6c `setTeam`, +0x70 `clearTeam`, +0x74 `getTeam`. Built: `viewer/vehicle-instance.js mayEnterHull`, `VehicleRegistry.enter`; `vehicle-entry.js nearestEntry`; `server/room-control.mjs onSeat` (features/vehicle-entry-team-rule) |
| SEAT-27 | **The hull's team is stamped by every occupant and cleared by the last one out; `dontClearTeamOnExit` keeps it, a `teamOnVehicle` spawn never loses it.** `PlayerControlObject+0x170` is the team, `+0x174` a reference count. `setTeam(t)`: team = t, count++. `clearTeam()`: count--, and at 0 team = 0 unless the PCO template's `+0x190` (`ObjectTemplate.dontClearTeamOnExit`, a bool) is set. `PlayerControlObject::enter` stamps the entrant's team on the ROOT for every seat; `exit(bool)` and `exitPlayer()` clear it on the root, and `GameServer::exitVehicle` runs exactly one of them per occupant. `ObjectSpawner::spawnObject` with `teamOnVehicle` calls `setTeam(spawner team)` with nobody aboard and nothing ever undoes it, so such a hull is its spawner's side for good. So a crewed hull is its crew's side, an empty one nobody's, a ship keeps its last crew's, a stamped spawn its spawner's | **confirmed** (2026-09-25) | ctor `0x083162b0`: template at `+0x10c` (`0x083162cc`), team and count zeroed (`0x08316462`, `0x0831646c`). `setTeam` `0x0831a5f0`, `clearTeam` `0x0831a610` (`cmpb $0x0,0x190(%eax)` on `[+0x10c]` at `0x0831a62d`). `enter` `0x08316f00`: `getBFPlayer([this+0x124])->+0x7c` then root IPCO `call *0x6c` `0x08317138`..`0x0831714d`. `exit(bool)` `0x08317d00`: root IPCO `call *0x70` `0x08318233`; `exitPlayer` `0x083183d0`: `0x083185fa`; `exitVehicle` `0x0814e2c0` calls IPCO vt+0x28 or vt+0x24 on one branch each. `spawnObject` `0x083140a0`: `if (template+0x185) call *0x6c(team +0x134)` `0x083143a8`; `handleFrameUpdate` `0x083138c0` only reads `getTeam`. No direct call to `0x0831a5f0`/`0x0831a610` or their thunks `0x0831ac40`/`0x0831ac50` in the whole disassembly. `dontClearTeamOnExit`: string `0x086d4c4f`, descriptor `0x087a91c0`, type `0x086b1e97` = "bool", `ConsoleClass511::executeObjectMethod` `0x082f4d60` writes `+0x190` of `getActiveTemplate(0xc4c2)` at `0x082f4d9c`. A team change kills the living player first (`GameServer::setTeam(BFPlayer*, int)` `0x08131800`). Data (vanilla + XPack1 + XPack2): `dontClearTeamOnExit 1` only on 12 ships (Enterprise, Fletcher, Fletcher2, Gato, Hatsuzuki, Hatsuzuki2, PrinceOW, Shokaku, Sub7C, Yamato; Coral Sea's Hiryu, Hornet); `teamOnVehicle` on no Berlin spawner, on every Kasserine Pass Conquest land and air spawner, and on the naval maps' ship spawners. Built: the crew stamp and the clear (`VehicleInstance.team`); the two persistence cases are NOT (the extract carries neither word) |
| SEAT-28 | **SEAT-4 corrected: the "second, independent team/hostility gate" is `Armor::isDestroyed`.** Both finders walk up to the entry point's Armor (`queryComponent(0xc4a4)`, which `Armor::queryInterface` answers with its primary `this`) and reject when its vt+0xc8 returns true; that slot is `Armor::isDestroyed()`. It is the wreck test, not a team test. `validateBFEntryPoint` has no team test at all; the team test a bot meets is `toggleEntryPoint`'s (SEAT-26) | **confirmed** (2026-09-25) | `Armor` vtable `0x0871d220` (vptr `0x0871d228`): +0xc8 = `Armor::isDestroyed()` `0x08174300`. `Armor::queryInterface` `0x08173ee0`: `cmp $0xc4a4,%edx` `0x08173ef3`, returns `this`. `validateBFEntryPoint` `0x0831d590` and `BFfindEntryPoint` `0x0831d770` both `call *0xc8` on that component, rejecting on true |
| SEAT-29 | **A bot neither weighs nor walks to a hull the enemy crews.** `BBChange::calculateUrgency` asks `BBChange::isMannedByEnemy` of every unit before scoring it and takes the next unit on true: the unit's side is not the bot's and `ai::vehicleOccupied` finds someone in the hull (its root or any seat under it). The side is the hull's crew's: `PlayerControlObject::enter` hands the root's AI object (`SimpleObject+0x58`) the entrant's team through `AIObject*::setSide`. The Change plan's walk also ends when the target becomes enemy-manned (`BAPConObjectEnemyOccupied`) | **confirmed** (2026-09-25) | `isMannedByEnemy` `0x0855fcb0` (`Information` vt+0x30 against the bot's side, then `vehicleOccupied` `0x08658ef0`), called at `0x0855ea07`, `je 0x0855ece6` scores only on false. AI object: `SimpleObject::SimpleObject` `AITemplate::createObject` `0x081da2cb` -> `setComponent` -> `BObject::setComponent` stores it at `+0x58` (`0x0816655a`); `enter` calls `(root+0x58)` vt+0x24 with `player+0x7c` at `0x0831706a`..`0x08317082`; `AIObjectPrimaryReal` vtable `0x08760da0`: +0x24 `setSide` `0x085d73a0`, +0x28 `getSide` `0x085d2640`; `InformationReal::getSide` `0x085e8da0`. Also read: `calculateVehicleUrgency` `0x08583b10` gives an occupied candidate seat (`isOccupied` `0x08583b75`) 0 unless `AIObjectUnit::getTeamLock` `0x085dc360` (the IPCO `getTeam`, `0x08583d7c`) is another side's; `BBPChange::createPlan` `0x0858b5c0` builds `BAPConObjectEnemyOccupied` at `0x0858bf85` (`evaluate` `0x08550670`). Built: `viewer/bot-vehicle.js mannedByEnemy`, both loops of `bot-mount.js urgencyChange`, `bot-units.js` candidates' `hullTeam`, `doctrine.js` `WPBoard`. Before: an Axis bot 9 m from the free MG seat of an Allied-driven T34-85 mounted it at 6.7 s; after: never weighed it (Berlin, `tests/vehicle_team_harness.mjs gate`) |
| CVM-1 | `CVMInside`/`CVMChase`/`CVMFrontChase`/`CVMFlyBy`/`CVMTrace`/`CVMExternTrace` are per-Camera booleans declaring which views (C) the seat offers; omission means on | **extracted** | Parsed by `con.py`, emitted as `extras.cameraView.cvm`, captured as `seat.cameraViewModes` in `viewer/seats.js`. Nothing in vanilla disables a mode, but FinnWars locks 67 cameras with `CVM* 0` flags |
